# Divergences from Federer's text

This register lists the places where the formalisation departs from the
printed text of Federer, *Geometric Measure Theory* (1969), in a statement
or in a proof. It is compiled from the section files indexed in
[the documentation index](toc.md) and from the `Source for`
comments in `theories/`; the entries there give the full arguments and
sources. Each row has one of the following kinds.

- **Proof route.** A different argument, auxiliary construction or order of
  proof steps from Federer's printed proof. Supporting expansions are described
  in the section accounts.
- **Statement correction.** A false or incomplete printed statement is
  corrected. The suffix *(erratum)* identifies Federer's May 1987 corrections,
  with the book page and line; *(local)* identifies a correction documented and
  verified in this project. The Rocq column records the proof or counterexample.
- **Hypothesis or convention.** A changed hypothesis, boundary convention,
  scalar field, dimension assumption or stronger supporting statement.
- **Interpretation.** An explicit reading of notation or an implicit
  convention needed to state the formal claim.

The following conventions hold throughout and are listed below only where
they change a particular statement.

- Every object is specified by a predicate expressing its universal
  property or defining laws; a construction only proves existence and is
  sealed. A row is given where Federer defines the object by a
  construction and his definition becomes a theorem.
- Chapter 1 works over an arbitrary field, or an ordered, real closed or
  complete real field where order, square roots or suprema are needed, in
  place of R. Characteristic hypotheses are listed per section.
- Finite dimension is witnessed by a finite basis, and every object built
  from a basis (Γ, traces, determinants, dual inner products, operator
  norms) is proved independent of it. Equalities between differently
  represented spaces, such as ⊗_0 V = R, ∧_1 V = V or Sym_0 V = R, are
  specified linear isomorphisms.
- Indices start at zero where Federer starts at one; truncated natural
  subtraction is avoided by rewriting dimension conditions additively.
- In Chapter 2 sets are predicates on choice types, a measure is defined on
  all subsets (Federer's convention), extended arithmetic is partial, and
  partial functions carry explicit domains. Total functions, such as
  functionals on a lattice L, have unspecified and irrelevant values
  outside their domain of definition.

Erratum entries that repair only typography or wording are followed
without comment and have no row: book p. 11 line 8, p. 15 line 17,
p. 20 lines 11 and 32 (∧_j V for the printed Λ_j V), p. 22 line 11,
p. 26 line 24, p. 59 line 3 (a ∈ α), p. 63 lines 2 and 30, p. 65 line 20,
p. 68 line 14 (the missing "of"), p. 76 line 3, p. 91 lines 10–11
(L_1(φ,Y) for L_(1)(φ,Y), and the proof heading of 2.4.19), p. 98 line 14,
p. 101 line 15 (x ∈ R^2) and p. 107 line 24 (the parenthesis in Φ(V)).

## 1.1 Tensor products

| Item | Kind | Divergence | Reason | Rocq |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1.1.1 | hypothesis or convention | Chapter 1 is formalised over an arbitrary field K in place of R, for vector spaces of arbitrary dimension; no real instance is supplied. | Federer notes on p. 8 that much of the theory holds more generally; no algebraic step needs real scalars. | [`is_tensor_product_family`](../theories/TensorFamilyUP.v#L39) ([`TensorFamilyUP.v`](../theories/TensorFamilyUP.v)), [`tensor_family_exists`](../theories/TensorFamilyConstruction.v#L181) ([`TensorFamilyConstruction.v`](../theories/TensorFamilyConstruction.v)) |
| 1.1.1 | hypothesis or convention | The tensor product of V_1, …, V_m is a predicate on m-linear maps on dependent finite functions; V ⊗ W is the case m = 2 of the family (V, W), proved equivalent to the binary interface. | One interface serves every number of factors. | [`is_tensor_product_family`](../theories/TensorFamilyUP.v#L39) ([`TensorFamilyUP.v`](../theories/TensorFamilyUP.v)), [`tensor_product_family_pair`](../theories/TensorFamilyBinary.v#L205) ([`TensorFamilyBinary.v`](../theories/TensorFamilyBinary.v)) |
| 1.1.2 | proof route | The basis of products of basis vectors of V ⊗ W is obtained, for arbitrary index sets, by lifting the product of the two coordinate maps through the universal property. Federer deduces it from V ⊗ W ≅ (P ⊗ W) ⊕ (Q ⊗ W). | The direct-sum identities are separately proved in [`TensorDirectSum.v`](../theories/TensorDirectSum.v); the basis proof does not depend on them. | [`tensor_basis_coordinates`](../theories/TensorBasis.v#L58), [`tensor_basisP`](../theories/TensorBasis.v#L118) ([`TensorBasis.v`](../theories/TensorBasis.v)) |
| 1.1.2, p. 10 | proof route | Prove arbitrary product bases independently using coordinate-functional products and finite induction through the arguments. Prove grouping by two successive universal extensions. | Expands the printed immediate consequences without assuming grouping, a product basis or finite-dimensional factors. | [`tensor_family_basisP`](../theories/TensorFamilyBasis.v#L142), [`tensor_family_grouping_isomorphism`](../theories/TensorFamilyGrouping.v#L153) |
| 1.1.2, p. 10 | hypothesis or convention | Permit zero blocks and zero total factor count in the supporting canonical maps and dimension formula. | Retains the original positive-factor assertions and checks the one-dimensional empty product and zero-dimensional factor boundaries. | [`tensor_family_grouping_isomorphism`](../theories/TensorFamilyGrouping.v#L153), [`tensor_family_dimension`](../theories/TensorFamilyDimension.v#L35) |
| 1.1.4, p. 11 | proof route | Prove unrestricted injectivity by finite support and finite evaluation separation; then use the printed finite target-basis expansion for surjectivity. | Expands the basis argument without choosing a global infinite basis. The finite-target inverse is proved linear and basis-independent; either finite factor suffices. | [`tensor_Hom_injective`](../theories/HomTensorGeneral.v#L53), [`Hom_tensor_finite_targetK`](../theories/HomTensorGeneral.v#L73), [`Hom_tensor_finite_target_basis_independent`](../theories/HomTensorGeneral.v#L122), [`tensor_Hom_finite_factor_surjective`](../theories/HomTensorGeneral.v#L108) |
| 1.1.4, p. 11, simultaneous-infinite exception | proof route | Finite coordinate support and countable retractions in both original infinite spaces give an actual map outside Phi. | Expands the printed exception into the exact surjectivity iff criterion; a finite-basis/infinite alternative is proved without assuming a global infinite basis. | [`tensor_Hom_infinite_missing_image`](../theories/HomTensorInfinite.v#L55), [`tensor_Hom_surjective_iff_finite_factor`](../theories/HomTensorInfinite.v#L99) ([`HomTensorInfinite.v`](../theories/HomTensorInfinite.v)) |

## 1.2 Graded algebras

| Item | Kind | Divergence | Reason | Rocq |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1.2.1 | hypothesis or convention | The specified direct-sum decomposition is a family of linear projections P_n of one space, with P_m ∘ P_n = 0 for m ≠ n, P_n ∘ P_n = P_n and finite reconstruction; the components are their images. | An internal direct sum on one module needs no external direct-sum construction. | [`grading`](../theories/GradedAlgebra.v#L24) ([`GradedAlgebra.v`](../theories/GradedAlgebra.v)), [`graded_component`](../theories/GradedComponents.v#L31) ([`GradedComponents.v`](../theories/GradedComponents.v)), [`graded_decomposition_unique`](../theories/GradedStructure.v#L56) ([`GradedStructure.v`](../theories/GradedStructure.v)) |
| 1.2.2 | hypothesis or convention | The decomposition of A ⊗ B into the spaces A_p ⊗ B_q is a grading of the binary tensor product by the projections Σ_(p ≤ n) P_p ⊗ Q_(n−p); no isomorphism with an external direct sum is constructed. | Keeps the projection form of 1.2.1. | [`tensor_grade_orthogonal`](../theories/GradedTensor.v#L160), [`tensor_grade_finite`](../theories/GradedTensor.v#L155), [`tensor_component_induction`](../theories/GradedTensor.v#L198) ([`GradedTensor.v`](../theories/GradedTensor.v)) |
| 1.2.3 | hypothesis or convention | The tensor algebra is specified by `is_graded_tensor_algebra`: a graded associative unital algebra with ι : V → A whose degree-m component, with the m-fold product of ι, is an m-fold tensor product of V. Federer defines ⊗_* V as the direct sum of the ⊗_m V. | Universal property first; later results use only the predicate. | [`graded_tensor_algebraP`](../theories/TensorAlgebraUP.v#L427) ([`TensorAlgebraUP.v`](../theories/TensorAlgebraUP.v)), [`tensor_algebraP`](../theories/TensorAlgebraSealed.v#L50) ([`TensorAlgebraSealed.v`](../theories/TensorAlgebraSealed.v)) |
| 1.2.3 | proof route | Existence is proved by a quotient of the finitely supported functions on words by the multilinearity relations, graded by word length. Federer forms the direct sum of the iterated tensor products and multiplies through the grouping isomorphisms. | The construction serves only to prove existence; the components are identified with the iterated products afterwards. | [`word_relation`](../theories/WordTensorAlgebra.v#L35) ([`WordTensorAlgebra.v`](../theories/WordTensorAlgebra.v)), [`word_tensor_algebra_exists`](../theories/WordTensorWitness.v#L78) ([`WordTensorWitness.v`](../theories/WordTensorWitness.v)), [`tensor_power_join`](../theories/TensorPowerProduct.v#L72) ([`TensorPowerProduct.v`](../theories/TensorPowerProduct.v)) |
| 1.2.3 | hypothesis or convention | The stronger ungraded property is proved as well: every linear map of V into an associative unital algebra extends uniquely to a unital algebra homomorphism. | Stronger formulation; the graded uniqueness theorem does not use it. | [`is_tensor_algebra`](../theories/AlgebraUP.v#L95), [`tensor_algebra_unique`](../theories/AlgebraUP.v#L136) ([`AlgebraUP.v`](../theories/AlgebraUP.v)) |

## 1.3 The exterior algebra of a vectorspace

| Item | Kind | Divergence | Reason | Rocq |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1.3.1 | hypothesis or convention | The exterior algebra is specified by [`is_exterior_power_algebra`](../theories/ExteriorAlgebraUP.v#L77) (graded, ι(v)ι(v) = 0, degree-m component universal for alternating m-linear maps). The quotient ⊗_* V / mV is only the existence witness, and the kernel of the canonical map is proved to be mV. Federer defines ∧_* V as this quotient. | Universal property first. | [`is_exterior_power_algebra`](../theories/ExteriorAlgebraUP.v#L77) ([`ExteriorAlgebraUP.v`](../theories/ExteriorAlgebraUP.v)), [`exterior_quotient_exists`](../theories/ExteriorQuotientWitness.v#L139) ([`ExteriorQuotientWitness.v`](../theories/ExteriorQuotientWitness.v)), [`exterior_projection_kernel`](../theories/ExteriorPowers.v#L83) ([`ExteriorPowers.v`](../theories/ExteriorPowers.v)) |
| 1.3.1 | hypothesis or convention | The ungraded square-zero universal property is proved in every characteristic, in addition to the printed graded anticommutative one. | Stronger formulation without a characteristic hypothesis. | [`is_exterior_algebra`](../theories/ExteriorUP.v#L35), [`exterior_algebra_unique`](../theories/ExteriorUP.v#L81) ([`ExteriorUP.v`](../theories/ExteriorUP.v)), [`exterior_power_algebraP`](../theories/ExteriorAlgebraUP.v#L359) ([`ExteriorAlgebraUP.v`](../theories/ExteriorAlgebraUP.v)) |
| 1.3.1–1.3.4 | hypothesis or convention | The graded universal property, the direct-sum isomorphism and basis theorems of 1.3.2, the diagonal Ψ of 1.3.3 and the determinant of 1.3.4 assume 1 + 1 ≠ 0 in K. | Federer derives a·a = 0 in degree one from anticommutativity, which needs characteristic different from two; this holds over R. | [`exterior_target_square_zero`](../theories/ExteriorMaps.v#L84) ([`ExteriorMaps.v`](../theories/ExteriorMaps.v)), [`degree_one_square_zero`](../theories/GradedAlgebra.v#L126) ([`GradedAlgebra.v`](../theories/GradedAlgebra.v)), [`beta_alpha`](../theories/ExteriorDirectSum.v#L266) ([`ExteriorDirectSum.v`](../theories/ExteriorDirectSum.v)), [`Psi`](../theories/ExteriorDiagonal.v#L32) ([`ExteriorDiagonal.v`](../theories/ExteriorDiagonal.v)) |
| 1.3.2 | interpretation | The basis "e_1, e_2, e_3, …" is read either as a finite basis or as a basis sequence indexed by the natural numbers; arbitrarily indexed ordered bases are not treated. | Matches the finite and sequential basis models implemented here. | [`finite_e_lambda_basis`](../theories/FiniteExteriorPowers.v#L218) ([`FiniteExteriorPowers.v`](../theories/FiniteExteriorPowers.v)), [`countable_e_lambda_basis`](../theories/CountableExteriorBasis.v#L380) ([`CountableExteriorBasis.v`](../theories/CountableExteriorBasis.v)) |
| 1.3.4 | hypothesis or convention | The determinant det(f) is defined from a chosen basis as the top coordinate of the image of e_1 ∧ … ∧ e_n; that it is the unique scalar with (∧_n f)(ξ) = det(f) ξ, and that it does not depend on the basis, are theorems. Federer defines det(f) directly as that scalar. | The basis theorem of 1.3.2 makes ∧_n V one-dimensional; uniqueness recovers the intrinsic definition. | [`det`](../theories/ExteriorDeterminant.v#L139), [`det_unique`](../theories/ExteriorDeterminant.v#L176), [`det_basis_independent`](../theories/ExteriorDeterminant.v#L239) ([`ExteriorDeterminant.v`](../theories/ExteriorDeterminant.v)) |
| 1.3.4 | hypothesis or convention | Permutations compose as right actions (σ * τ applies σ first), so the homomorphism property reads φ(σ * τ) = φ(τ) ∘ φ(σ). | MathComp's permutation-group convention. | [`phi`](../theories/PermutationDeterminant.v#L35), [`phi_comp`](../theories/PermutationDeterminant.v#L51), [`index_multiplicative`](../theories/PermutationDeterminant.v#L139) ([`PermutationDeterminant.v`](../theories/PermutationDeterminant.v)) |

## 1.4 Alternating forms and duality

| Item | Kind | Divergence | Reason | Rocq |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1.4.1 | hypothesis or convention | The space A^*(V,W) consists of the linear maps on the exterior algebra with finitely many nonzero homogeneous restrictions, the model in which 1.4.2 defines the product, rather than the direct sum of the A^m(V,W); inclusions and components identify the two. | The product of 1.4.2 is defined on this model. | [`A_star`](../theories/AlternatingSum.v#L32), [`form_include`](../theories/AlternatingSum.v#L44), [`form_component`](../theories/AlternatingSum.v#L50), [`alternating_sum_decomposition`](../theories/AlternatingSum.v#L128) ([`AlternatingSum.v`](../theories/AlternatingSum.v)) |
| 1.4.1–1.4.4 | hypothesis or convention | The alternating product (1.4.2) and everything built on it assume 1 + 1 ≠ 0: the top-degree example of 1.4.1, the bases of 1.4.3 and Ω in 1.4.4, including the coefficient statements. The remaining assertions of 1.4.1 hold over any field. | The alternating product uses the diagonal Ψ of 1.3.3. | [`alternating_wedge`](../theories/AlternatingProduct.v#L44) ([`AlternatingProduct.v`](../theories/AlternatingProduct.v)), [`omega_lambda_basis`](../theories/GrassmannCoordinates.v#L87) ([`GrassmannCoordinates.v`](../theories/GrassmannCoordinates.v)), [`Omega`](../theories/OmegaConstruction.v#L136) ([`OmegaConstruction.v`](../theories/OmegaConstruction.v)) |
| 1.4.2 | hypothesis or convention | The iterated shuffle formula for m forms is proved for the right-associated product without associativity of the coefficient multiplication. | The induction does not use associativity; the associative case follows. | [`forms_product`](../theories/AlternatingFamily.v#L111) ([`AlternatingFamily.v`](../theories/AlternatingFamily.v)), [`iterated_form_shuffle`](../theories/IteratedShuffle.v#L115) ([`IteratedShuffle.v`](../theories/IteratedShuffle.v)) |
| 1.4.2 | hypothesis or convention | Without a coefficient unit the empty product of forms is not defined, and those statements exclude the empty family; with a unit the empty product is the unit and the shuffle formula holds also for m = 0. | An empty product needs a unit. | [`nonempty_product`](../theories/NonemptyProduct.v#L26) ([`NonemptyProduct.v`](../theories/NonemptyProduct.v)), [`unital_forms_product`](../theories/UnitalFamily.v#L47), [`unital_iterated_form_shuffle`](../theories/UnitalFamily.v#L107) ([`UnitalFamily.v`](../theories/UnitalFamily.v)) |
| 1.4.4 | proof route | Injectivity of Ω for arbitrary V reduces each element to finitely many covectors, recovered from finitely many evaluations, and then applies the finite-dimensional argument. Federer chooses a basis of Hom(V,R). | Avoids a basis of an arbitrary dual space. | [`Omega_injective`](../theories/OmegaInjective.v#L62) ([`OmegaInjective.v`](../theories/OmegaInjective.v)), [`exterior_finite_support`](../theories/ExteriorFiniteSupport.v#L28) ([`ExteriorFiniteSupport.v`](../theories/ExteriorFiniteSupport.v)), [`finite_evaluation_reconstruction`](../theories/FiniteEvaluation.v#L70) ([`FiniteEvaluation.v`](../theories/FiniteEvaluation.v)) |
| 1.4.4 | proof route | Federer states without proof that im Ω omits elements of degree m ≥ 2 when dim V = ∞. | Avoids choosing a basis of V. | [`Omega_infinite_missing_degree`](../theories/OmegaInfinite.v#L105), [`infinite_block_not_in_image`](../theories/OmegaInfinite.v#L93) ([`OmegaInfinite.v`](../theories/OmegaInfinite.v)), [`countable_retractionK`](../theories/CountableRetraction.v#L95) ([`CountableRetraction.v`](../theories/CountableRetraction.v)) |
| 1.4.4 | statement correction (local) | The coefficient parenthesis claims, for every associative unital W, a homomorphism from the exterior algebra of Hom(V,W) to A^*(V,W) extending the identity on one-forms. This is false for V = K^2 and the free algebra W = K⟨X,Y⟩, where (α ∧ α)(e_1, e_2) = XY − YX ≠ 0; existence and uniqueness are proved for commutative W. | Such an extension forces all values of a one-form to commute. | [`noncommutative_exterior_extension_impossible`](../theories/NoncommutativeObstruction.v#L108) ([`NoncommutativeObstruction.v`](../theories/NoncommutativeObstruction.v)), [`Omega_W`](../theories/OmegaConstruction.v#L53), [`Omega_W_unique`](../theories/OmegaConstruction.v#L109) ([`OmegaConstruction.v`](../theories/OmegaConstruction.v)) |
| 1.4.4 | statement correction (local) | "Not injective unless dim W = 1" fails for V = 0 and W = K × K, where the map c ↦ (c, c) is injective. The corrected statement for nonzero V is argued in the documentation only. | The printed text does not exclude the zero space. | [`zero_domain_Omega_pair_injective`](../theories/ZeroDimensionalCoefficients.v#L78) ([`ZeroDimensionalCoefficients.v`](../theories/ZeroDimensionalCoefficients.v)) |
| 1.4.4 | statement correction (local) | "Surjective whenever dim V < ∞" fails for every V: the degree-zero values lie in K·1_W, so for W = K × K the constant zero-form (1, 0) has no preimage. Surjectivity forces W = K·1_W. | The degree-zero source is K, the degree-zero target is W. | [`coefficient_pair_not_surjective`](../theories/CoefficientCounterexample.v#L114) ([`CoefficientCounterexample.v`](../theories/CoefficientCounterexample.v)), [`Omega_W_surjective_requires_scalar_coefficients`](../theories/CoefficientImage.v#L72) ([`CoefficientImage.v`](../theories/CoefficientImage.v)) |
| 1.4.4 | hypothesis or convention | The exterior algebra over a commutative W ("constructed with W replacing R") is specified by an ungraded universal property with central coefficients and a W-linear square-zero generator, and constructed as a quotient of W ⊗ ∧_* Hom(V,W); W may have zero divisors. | Reuses the exterior algebra over K instead of repeating the construction over the ring W. | [`is_relative_exterior_algebra`](../theories/RelativeExterior.v#L37), [`relative_exterior_exists`](../theories/RelativeExterior.v#L351) ([`RelativeExterior.v`](../theories/RelativeExterior.v)), [`Omega_relative_unique`](../theories/RelativeOmega.v#L85) ([`RelativeOmega.v`](../theories/RelativeOmega.v)) |
| 1.4.5 | statement correction (local) | The exception "(unless V ≅ R or W ≅ R)" is refuted for V = W = 0: the ordinary tensor product of the two exterior algebras is then commutative. It is also refuted, by an argument not formalised, for V = 0 and dim W ≥ 2: the product is then isomorphic to the exterior algebra of W, which is anticommutative in the sense of 1.2.1. The sharp exception is "(unless V = 0, or W = 0, or V ≅ R ≅ W)". | The printed exception omits zero spaces. | [`zero_ordinary_tensor_commutative`](../theories/OrdinaryTensorException.v#L84), [`zero_space_not_isomorphic_scalar`](../theories/OrdinaryTensorException.v#L110) ([`OrdinaryTensorException.v`](../theories/OrdinaryTensorException.v)) |
| 1.4.5 | statement correction (local) | That the ordinary tensor product is neither commutative nor anticommutative is proved when each of V, W contains two independent vectors, including infinite dimension. | Two independent vectors in each factor supply the explicit obstructions. | [`ordinary_tensor_neither`](../theories/OrdinaryTensorDimension.v#L82) ([`OrdinaryTensorDimension.v`](../theories/OrdinaryTensorDimension.v)), [`ordinary_tensor_not_commutative`](../theories/OrdinaryTensorObstruction.v#L129), [`ordinary_tensor_not_anticommutative`](../theories/OrdinaryTensorObstruction.v#L176) ([`OrdinaryTensorObstruction.v`](../theories/OrdinaryTensorObstruction.v)) |
| 1.4.5 | hypothesis or convention | The contraction formula with Γ(1 on ∧_j V) is proved for every j, not only for j ≤ n − m. | The binomial coefficient vanishes for larger j. | [`trace_contraction`](../theories/TraceContraction.v#L141) ([`TraceContraction.v`](../theories/TraceContraction.v)) |
| 1.4.5 | hypothesis or convention | Exterior products need 1 + 1 ≠ 0; divided powers need m! ≠ 0, and the formula is first proved with m! multiplied out; the determinant and characteristic-polynomial formulae assume characteristic zero. | Division by factorials. | [`Gamma_power_factorial`](../theories/DividedPowers.v#L39), [`Gamma_divided_power_formula`](../theories/DividedPowers.v#L71) ([`DividedPowers.v`](../theories/DividedPowers.v)), [`characteristic_polynomialE`](../theories/CharacteristicPolynomial.v#L131) ([`CharacteristicPolynomial.v`](../theories/CharacteristicPolynomial.v)) |
| 1.4.5 | interpretation | In trace(∧^1 f) = trace(f) the printed superscript is kept: ∧^1 f is the dual map A^1 f on one-covectors of 1.4.1, not the first exterior power of f. | The book prints a superscript, and this reading is not a tautology. | [`trace_dual`](../theories/TraceDual.v#L67), [`one_covector_dualE`](../theories/TraceDual.v#L50) ([`TraceDual.v`](../theories/TraceDual.v)) |

## 1.6 Simple m-vectors

| Item | Kind | Divergence | Reason | Rocq |

| 1.6.1–1.6.6 | hypothesis or convention | Arbitrary field of characteristic different from two in place of R; positive proportionality and G_o(V,m) (1.6.2) over an ordered field. In 1.6.6, R is an ordered field and C any field with an embedding, imaginary unit, real and imaginary parts satisfying [`is_complex_scalar_system`](../theories/ComplexScalarUP.v#L17). | Inherited from 1.3.1; positivity needs only an order; completeness and algebraic closure are not used. | [`simple`](../theories/AssociatedSubspace.v#L27) ([`AssociatedSubspace.v`](../theories/AssociatedSubspace.v)), [`positive_equivalent`](../theories/PositiveEquivalence.v#L30) ([`PositiveEquivalence.v`](../theories/PositiveEquivalence.v)), [`is_complex_scalar_system`](../theories/ComplexScalarUP.v#L17) ([`ComplexScalarUP.v`](../theories/ComplexScalarUP.v)) |
| 1.6.1 | statement correction (local) | "A nonzero m-vector is simple if and only if dim T = m" holds only for m > 0; for m = 0 only the forward implication holds. | 1.6.2 | interpretation | The "Grassmann manifold" G(V,m) is the set of subspaces with an m-element basis, without finite dimension of V, and the "identification space" G_o(V,m) a quotient type of nonzero simple m-vectors; no topology is formalised in this item. | The item makes only set-level assertions. | [`grassmann_pred`](../theories/GrassmannSubspaces.v#L32) ([`GrassmannSubspaces.v`](../theories/GrassmannSubspaces.v)), [`oriented_rel`](../theories/OrientedGrassmann.v#L33) ([`OrientedGrassmann.v`](../theories/OrientedGrassmann.v)) |

| 1.6.3 | hypothesis or convention | The annihilator identities and the intersection corollary keep all degrees, including n = 0, through the auxiliary notion of a nonzero multiple of a simple covector. The condition dim(T ∩ U) = p + r − n is stated as k + n = p + r. | Literal simplicity fails in degree zero; truncated subtraction would falsify the printed equation when p + r < n. | [`scaled_simple_covector`](../theories/ScaledSimpleCovectors.v#L28) ([`ScaledSimpleCovectors.v`](../theories/ScaledSimpleCovectors.v)), [`D_p_associated_annihilator`](../theories/DualityAnnihilator.v#L87) ([`DualityAnnihilator.v`](../theories/DualityAnnihilator.v)), [`intersection_dimension_iff_dual_product`](../theories/IntersectionDuality.v#L62) ([`IntersectionDuality.v`](../theories/IntersectionDuality.v)) |
| 1.6.3 | hypothesis or convention | Transport of simplicity through Ω and the identity for the annihilator of an intersection are proved for V of arbitrary dimension. | Neither needs finite dimension. | [`simple_covector_Omega`](../theories/SimpleCovectors.v#L53) ([`SimpleCovectors.v`](../theories/SimpleCovectors.v)), [`annihilator_intersection`](../theories/AnnihilatorIntersection.v#L80) ([`AnnihilatorIntersection.v`](../theories/AnnihilatorIntersection.v)) |
| 1.6.5 | hypothesis or convention | The identity is proved on the whole exterior algebra, for an arbitrary top form and all degrees, without simplicity or transversality; the degree-specific form assumes k, l ≤ n explicitly. The change of dual bases is formalised as a pullback by any endomorphism f, which multiplies both members by det(f). | Stronger formulation by linearity; the bounds type the maps D_k and D_l. | [`product_space_contraction_hom`](../theories/ProductSpaceIdentity.v#L26), [`product_space_contraction`](../theories/ProductSpaceIdentity.v#L65), [`product_space_volume_change`](../theories/ProductSpaceIdentity.v#L108) ([`ProductSpaceIdentity.v`](../theories/ProductSpaceIdentity.v)) |

| Item | Kind | Divergence | Reason | Rocq |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1.7.1–1.7.13 | hypothesis or convention | Algebraic results hold over ordered or real closed fields; a complete real field is assumed only where compactness, suprema or completeness are used. Over real closed fields the dual norm of 1.7.2 is an attained bound, and the supremum formula is proved over a complete field. | Suprema need not exist without completeness, but attainment suffices. | [`is_inner_product`](../theories/InnerProductUP.v#L24) ([`InnerProductUP.v`](../theories/InnerProductUP.v)), [`inner_dual_norm_attained`](../theories/InnerDualNorm.v#L53) ([`InnerDualNorm.v`](../theories/InnerDualNorm.v)), [`inner_dual_norm_supremum`](../theories/InnerDualSupremum.v#L33) ([`InnerDualSupremum.v`](../theories/InnerDualSupremum.v)) |
| 1.7.3 | proof route | The vanishing of S(e_i, e_j) for i < j is proved algebraically: S(x,x)B − S is positive semidefinite on the constraint subspace and vanishes at the maximiser. Federer uses the normalised curve through e_i + t e_j. | Avoids differentiation. | [`rayleigh_stationary_in_subspace`](../theories/RayleighStationary.v#L17) ([`RayleighStationary.v`](../theories/RayleighStationary.v)), [`semidefinite_zero_diagonal_in_subspace`](../theories/SemidefiniteRestriction.v#L33) ([`SemidefiniteRestriction.v`](../theories/SemidefiniteRestriction.v)) |

| 1.7.4 | hypothesis or convention | The elementary adjoint identities hold over an ordered field for any maps satisfying the adjoint equation, without finite dimension. | 1.7.6 | hypothesis or convention | The operator norm is specified by a bound on the closed unit ball and attainment there; the supremum formula is a theorem. | Item | Kind | Divergence | Reason | Rocq |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1.8.1 | statement correction (local) | Comass, mass and all norm assertions are stated for positive degree. | 1.8.1 | statement correction (local) | The representation of the mass unit ball by sums of c_i ξ_i with c_i > 0, Σ c_i = 1 and at most binom(dim V, m) terms assumes binom(dim V, m) > 0; the hull equality and the optimal decomposition hold also when ∧_m V = 0. | With a zero-dimensional exterior power the coefficients cannot sum to one. | [`mass_positive_convex_representation`](../theories/MassPositiveConvexRepresentation.v#L21), [`zero_dimensional_positive_convex_impossible`](../theories/MassPositiveConvexRepresentation.v#L76) ([`MassPositiveConvexRepresentation.v`](../theories/MassPositiveConvexRepresentation.v)) |
| 1.8.1 | hypothesis or convention | The lower comparison is stated as: the Euclidean norm is at most binom(n,m)^(1/2) times the comass. | Avoids inverting a zero binomial coefficient when m > n | [`euclidean_le_comass`](../theories/ComassComparison.v#L15) ([`ComassComparison.v`](../theories/ComassComparison.v)) |
| 1.8.1 | proof route | The convex-set facts that Federer cites from [BF](bibliography.md#bonnesenfenchel1934), [BO](bibliography.md#bourbaki) and [EG 2](bibliography.md#eggleston1963) are proved locally: elimination of coefficients along a linear relation, compactness of the hull, and separation by the variational inequality of a nearest point . | No external convexity theorem is imported. | [`subconvex_dimension_reduction`](../theories/SubconvexReduction.v#L27) ([`SubconvexReduction.v`](../theories/SubconvexReduction.v)), [`compact_inner_nearest_exists`](../theories/CompactNearest.v#L22) ([`CompactNearest.v`](../theories/CompactNearest.v)), [`inner_nearest_convex_variational`](../theories/NearestConvexVariational.v#L17) ([`NearestConvexVariational.v`](../theories/NearestConvexVariational.v)), [`mass_unit_ball_convex_hull`](../theories/MassConvexHull.v#L93) ([`MassConvexHull.v`](../theories/MassConvexHull.v)) |
| 1.8.2 | hypothesis or convention | Wirtinger's inequality and its equality case are proved on Hermitian spaces of arbitrary complex dimension; μ ≤ dim_C V is expressed by an independent complex μ-frame. Degree μ = 0 is kept with the literal empty product. | No finite dimension is needed, and the degree-zero norm convention refuted in 1.8.1 is not assumed. | [`wirtinger_inequality`](../theories/Wirtinger.v#L26), [`wirtinger_equality`](../theories/Wirtinger.v#L35) ([`Wirtinger.v`](../theories/Wirtinger.v)), [`wirtinger_comass_specification`](../theories/WirtingerComass.v#L43) ([`WirtingerComass.v`](../theories/WirtingerComass.v)), [`two_form_zeroth_comass`](../theories/WirtingerZeroDegree.v#L46) ([`WirtingerZeroDegree.v`](../theories/WirtingerZeroDegree.v)) |
| 1.8.3 | hypothesis or convention | The comass unit ball and its convexity are formalised for any inner-product space; the printed set in ∧^m R^n is an instance. | Generalisation. | [`comass_unit_ball_convex`](../theories/ComassUnitBall.v#L42) ([`ComassUnitBall.v`](../theories/ComassUnitBall.v)), [`euclidean_comass_unit_ball_convex`](../theories/EuclideanComassBall.v#L32) ([`EuclideanComassBall.v`](../theories/EuclideanComassBall.v)) |
| 1.8.4 | interpretation | "An inner product space V" is read with the finite dimension stipulated in 1.8.1, and both degrees are positive. | The item does not repeat the standing hypothesis; mass is a norm only in positive degree. | [`orthogonal_mass_product`](../theories/OrthogonalMass.v#L20) ([`OrthogonalMass.v`](../theories/OrthogonalMass.v)) |
| 1.8.4 | proof route | The printed proof chooses orthonormal ω_1, …, ω_p and ψ with comass(ψ) = 1 and ⟨η, ψ⟩ = mass(η); these choices fail in degenerate cases, for instance above the ambient dimension, where every covector is zero. The formal proof uses a maximiser on the closed comass ball and treats a zero factor separately. | The printed auxiliary choice is not always possible. | [`mass_attained`](../theories/MassAttainment.v#L107) ([`MassAttainment.v`](../theories/MassAttainment.v)), [`positive_mass_attained_on_comass_sphere`](../theories/MassUnitAttainment.v#L19) ([`MassUnitAttainment.v`](../theories/MassUnitAttainment.v)), [`no_unit_comass_above_dimension`](../tests/ExteriorExamples.v#L1519) ([`ExteriorExamples.v`](../tests/ExteriorExamples.v)) |

Products need only a bilinear coefficient multiplication, and the coordinate expansion holds for any finite family of vectors. | Division by factorials. | [`S_m`](../theories/SymmetricForms.v#L30) ([`SymmetricForms.v`](../theories/SymmetricForms.v)), [`symmetric_dual_monomial_basis`](../theories/SymmetricDualMonomialBasis.v#L65) ([`SymmetricDualMonomialBasis.v`](../theories/SymmetricDualMonomialBasis.v)), [`homogeneous_polynomial_unique_representation`](../theories/HomogeneousPolynomialFunctions.v#L117) ([`HomogeneousPolynomialFunctions.v`](../theories/HomogeneousPolynomialFunctions.v)), [`polynomial_coordinates`](../theories/PolynomialCoordinates.v#L56) ([`PolynomialCoordinates.v`](../theories/PolynomialCoordinates.v)) |
| 1.10.1 | hypothesis or convention | The direct sum of the spaces of m-forms is realised as the linear maps on the symmetric algebra vanishing on all but finitely many homogeneous components. | Model proved isomorphic to the direct sum. | [`S_star`](../theories/SymmetricFormSum.v#L22), [`symmetric_form_sum_decomposition`](../theories/SymmetricFormSum.v#L75) ([`SymmetricFormSum.v`](../theories/SymmetricFormSum.v)) |
| 1.10.4 | proof route | The correspondence between the symmetric product and pointwise multiplication is proved with the divided-power diagonal of 1.9.4 instead of the calculation with card Sh(p,q). | Reuses 1.9.4. | [`homogeneous_polynomial_product`](../theories/SymmetricPolynomialProduct.v#L29), [`symmetric_polynomial_product_value`](../theories/SymmetricPolynomialProduct.v#L58) ([`SymmetricPolynomialProduct.v`](../theories/SymmetricPolynomialProduct.v)) |
| 1.10.4 | proof route | The identity for S_i(x + v) follows from the composition law of Taylor translations, derived from uniqueness of polynomial representations, instead of the double-sum rearrangement. | Uniqueness is already proved. | [`symmetric_taylor_transform_add`](../theories/PolynomialTaylorTransform.v#L153), [`symmetric_taylor_coefficient_translation`](../theories/PolynomialTaylorTransform.v#L165) ([`PolynomialTaylorTransform.v`](../theories/PolynomialTaylorTransform.v)) |
| 1.10.5 | hypothesis or convention | Norms of forms and polynomials are extended-real suprema, so unbounded forms are allowed, with 0·∞ = 0; several results assume only seminorms on the input or coefficient spaces. | Extended values keep the definition total; the proofs use only the seminorm laws. | [`is_symmetric_operator_norm`](../theories/SymmetricOperatorNormUP.v#L37) ([`SymmetricOperatorNormUP.v`](../theories/SymmetricOperatorNormUP.v)), [`is_polynomial_norm`](../theories/PolynomialNormUP.v#L31) ([`PolynomialNormUP.v`](../theories/PolynomialNormUP.v)), [`symmetric_operator_product_bound`](../theories/SymmetricOperatorProductBound.v#L61) ([`SymmetricOperatorProductBound.v`](../theories/SymmetricOperatorProductBound.v)) |
| 1.10.5 | hypothesis or convention | The identity for the norm of φ^k is proved for right-associated powers under the power-norm law alone, without associativity or unit; the monomial bounds assume abs(e_i) ≤ 1 and norm(ω_i) ≤ 1 in place of equalities, and include degree zero. | Stronger formulations; the proofs use only these laws. | [`symmetric_one_form_power_norm`](../theories/SymmetricOneFormPowerNorm.v#L87) ([`SymmetricOneFormPowerNorm.v`](../theories/SymmetricOneFormPowerNorm.v)), [`symmetric_dual_monomial_norm_upper`](../theories/SymmetricMonomialNormBounds.v#L42), [`symmetric_monomial_mass_lower`](../theories/SymmetricMonomialNormBounds.v#L74) ([`SymmetricMonomialNormBounds.v`](../theories/SymmetricMonomialNormBounds.v)) |
| 1.10.5 | statement correction (local) | The infimum formula for mass over finite sums of products of m vectors, and the bound on the number of terms, hold only for m > 0; degree-zero mass is the absolute value. No finite sum of empty products equals −1. | The unweighted formula fails in degree zero | [`symmetric_negative_unit_not_empty_sum`](../theories/SymmetricZeroDegreeDecomposition.v#L29) ([`SymmetricZeroDegreeDecomposition.v`](../theories/SymmetricZeroDegreeDecomposition.v)), [`symmetric_degree_zero_mass_specification`](../theories/SymmetricDegreeZeroMass.v#L48) ([`SymmetricDegreeZeroMass.v`](../theories/SymmetricDegreeZeroMass.v)) |
| 1.10.5 | proof route | The equality m!·norm(P) = norm(φ) for inner-product inputs, which Federer cites from [H 1], is proved locally: compactness gives a maximising unit tuple, polarisation and the parallelogram identity make its entries equal, a norming functional reduces to scalars, and restriction to a finite span removes the dimension hypothesis. | A proof is supplied instead of a citation; it does not claim to reproduce Hörmander's argument. | [`hilbert_polynomial_norm_equality`](../theories/HilbertPolynomialNorm.v#L55) ([`HilbertPolynomialNorm.v`](../theories/HilbertPolynomialNorm.v)), [`finite_hilbert_diagonal_maximum`](../theories/FiniteHilbertDiagonalMaximum.v#L22) ([`FiniteHilbertDiagonalMaximum.v`](../theories/FiniteHilbertDiagonalMaximum.v)) |
| 1.10.6 | hypothesis or convention | The algebraic and inner-product results hold over real closed fields, and every degree including zero is retained; only the operator suprema use a complete field. | No completeness is needed elsewhere. | [`symmetric_form_norm_square_sum`](../theories/SymmetricFormInnerCoordinates.v#L71) ([`SymmetricFormInnerCoordinates.v`](../theories/SymmetricFormInnerCoordinates.v)), [`symmetric_form_polynomial_hilbert_bound`](../theories/SymmetricFormHilbertBounds.v#L56) ([`SymmetricFormHilbertBounds.v`](../theories/SymmetricFormHilbertBounds.v)) |
| 1.10.6 | statement correction (local) | In the fibre condition α(j) = card{i : s(i) = j} the index j ranges over 1, …, n, not the printed 1, …, m. | Misprint; no erratum entry. | [`symmetric_prescribed_fibre_condition`](../theories/SymmetricFunctionFibres.v#L29) ([`SymmetricFunctionFibres.v`](../theories/SymmetricFunctionFibres.v)), [`symmetric_fibre_cardinality`](../theories/SymmetricWordFibreCount.v#L118) ([`SymmetricWordFibreCount.v`](../theories/SymmetricWordFibreCount.v)) |

## 2.1 Measures and measurable sets

| Item | Kind | Divergence | Reason | Rocq |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2.1.1 | hypothesis or convention | Sets, including index sets of sums, are predicates on choice types and need not be countable or inhabited; the scalar field is an abstract complete Archimedean field. | Typed modelling of Federer's sets; every complete Archimedean field is isomorphic to R. | [`is_nonnegative_sum`](../theories/NonnegativeSumUP.v#L18) ([`NonnegativeSumUP.v`](../theories/NonnegativeSumUP.v)), [`is_measure`](../theories/MeasureUP.v#L20) ([`MeasureUP.v`](../theories/MeasureUP.v)) |
| 2.1.1 | hypothesis or convention | Extended arithmetic is partial: ∞ − ∞, 0·∞ and undefined signed sums have the value `None` of an option type, distinct from both infinities; the convention 0·∞ = 0 is a separate operation used only where Federer indicates it. | Federer leaves these expressions undefined. | [`extended_add`](../theories/ExtendedRealPartial.v#L16), [`extended_multiply`](../theories/ExtendedRealPartial.v#L27), [`extended_zero_product`](../theories/ExtendedRealPartial.v#L33) ([`ExtendedRealPartial.v`](../theories/ExtendedRealPartial.v)), [`numerical_sum`](../theories/NumericalSum.v#L18) ([`NumericalSum.v`](../theories/NumericalSum.v)) |
| 2.1.1(8) | interpretation | The premise "−∞ < Σ f or Σ g < ∞" is read as definedness of that sum with the bound; the conclusion asserts that both sums are defined and ordered. | The printed rule does not say which sum is known to exist. | [`numerical_sum_comparison`](../theories/NumericalSumOrder.v#L46) ([`NumericalSumOrder.v`](../theories/NumericalSumOrder.v)) |
| 2.1.1(9)–(11), (13) | interpretation | The grouping, iterated-sum and exhaustion identities include definedness of every inner, iterated or restricted sum; equality of the signed totals in (11) is equality of partial values, both possibly undefined. | These identities presuppose that the inner sums exist; (11) has no definedness premise. | [`numerical_sum_grouping`](../theories/NumericalSumGrouping.v#L20) ([`NumericalSumGrouping.v`](../theories/NumericalSumGrouping.v)), [`numerical_sum_product_first`](../theories/NumericalSumProduct.v#L31) ([`NumericalSumProduct.v`](../theories/NumericalSumProduct.v)), [`numerical_sum_weighted_product`](../theories/NumericalSumWeightedProduct.v#L38) ([`NumericalSumWeightedProduct.v`](../theories/NumericalSumWeightedProduct.v)), [`numerical_sum_increasing_exhaustion`](../theories/NumericalSumContinuity.v#L21) ([`NumericalSumContinuity.v`](../theories/NumericalSumContinuity.v)) |
| 2.1.2 | hypothesis or convention | Counting measure is specified by the law μ(A) = Σ_(x ∈ A) 1 rather than by the number of elements; the final equivalence for images of restricted measures is proved for an arbitrary set function. | Universal property first; only the splitting identities are used. | [`is_counting_measure`](../theories/CountingMeasureUP.v#L19) ([`CountingMeasureUP.v`](../theories/CountingMeasureUP.v)), [`measure_pushforward_restrictions_iff`](../theories/MeasurePushforward.v#L36) ([`MeasurePushforward.v`](../theories/MeasurePushforward.v)) |
| 2.1.3(2), (3) | hypothesis or convention | Closure under countable unions and intersections, and countable additivity, are proved for arbitrary countable indexed families, including the empty family, whose intersection is the whole space. | Generalisation to empty and arbitrary countable index sets. | [`measure_measurable_countable_union`](../theories/MeasureCountableClosure.v#L61), [`measure_measurable_countable_intersection`](../theories/MeasureCountableClosure.v#L94) ([`MeasureCountableClosure.v`](../theories/MeasureCountableClosure.v)), [`measure_countable_disjoint_additivity`](../theories/MeasureCountableAdditivity.v#L20) ([`MeasureCountableAdditivity.v`](../theories/MeasureCountableAdditivity.v)) |
| 2.1.3(2) | proof route | Change of proof order: countable unions are shown measurable directly after finite closure, by passing the finite splitting bounds to the numerical sum. Federer first proves (3)–(5) and derives (2) from (4) and (5) applied to φ restricted to T with φ(T) < ∞; (5) is then proved by the book's argument. | Avoids the simultaneous limit of the two test-set pieces. | [`measure_measurable_sequence_union`](../theories/MeasureCountableClosure.v#L24) ([`MeasureCountableClosure.v`](../theories/MeasureCountableClosure.v)), [`measure_continuity_above`](../theories/MeasureContinuityAbove.v#L21) ([`MeasureContinuityAbove.v`](../theories/MeasureContinuityAbove.v)) |
| 2.1.5 | statement correction (erratum) | The example failing (5) uses φ(A) = card(A) for A ⊂ S (erratum p. 1, book p. 57 line 26); the book prints card(S). | The printed formula does not define a measure. | [`two_point_restriction_measure`](../theories/TwoPointRestrictionMeasure.v#L43) ([`TwoPointRestrictionMeasure.v`](../theories/TwoPointRestrictionMeasure.v)), [`two_point_restriction_classification_counterexample`](../theories/TwoPointRestrictionCounterexample.v#L101) ([`TwoPointRestrictionCounterexample.v`](../theories/TwoPointRestrictionCounterexample.v)) |
| 2.1.6 | hypothesis or convention | Cardinals are represented by sets compared by injections, not by von Neumann cardinals; the Ulam property is tested on a subset of an ambient type and proved invariant under equipollence; (1) is a union statement over representatives, and successors and accessibility are specified by certificates. | No ordinal model is constructed. | [`is_ulam_set`](../theories/UlamCardinalUP.v#L24), [`ulam_set_cardinal_invariant`](../theories/UlamCardinalUP.v#L44) ([`UlamCardinalUP.v`](../theories/UlamCardinalUP.v)), [`is_cardinal_successor`](../theories/CardinalSuccessorUP.v#L19) ([`CardinalSuccessorUP.v`](../theories/CardinalSuccessorUP.v)), [`ulam_set_union`](../theories/UlamUnion.v#L26) ([`UlamUnion.v`](../theories/UlamUnion.v)), [`is_accessible_cardinal`](../theories/UlamAccessibility.v#L23) ([`UlamAccessibility.v`](../theories/UlamAccessibility.v)) |
| 2.1.6(2) | proof route | Federer bounds the pairs (b, a) with φ U(b, a) > 0 by ℵ_0·α = α < β and picks a null row; the formal proof shows that the projection of these pairs is φ null by the Ulam null-cover theorem. | The proved null-cover property replaces the cardinal-product estimate. | [`ulam_small_prefix_measure_zero`](../theories/UlamSuccessorMatrix.v#L30) ([`UlamSuccessorMatrix.v`](../theories/UlamSuccessorMatrix.v)), [`ulam_null_cover`](../theories/UlamNullCover.v#L26) ([`UlamNullCover.v`](../theories/UlamNullCover.v)) |
| 2.1.6(2) | proof route | In place of von Neumann's model (X = β, each x an ordinal injected into α) the proof uses a representative from the well-ordering principle whose initial segments are bounded by the predecessor; finite and empty predecessors are included, whereas Federer assumes α infinite. | No ordinal model is constructed. | [`ulam_set_successor`](../theories/UlamSuccessor.v#L22) ([`UlamSuccessor.v`](../theories/UlamSuccessor.v)), [`well_order_prefix`](../theories/CardinalSuccessorUP.v#L27) ([`CardinalSuccessorUP.v`](../theories/CardinalSuccessorUP.v)) |

## 2.2 Borel and Suslin sets

| Item | Kind | Divergence | Reason | Rocq |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2.2.1 | hypothesis or convention | Borel families are predicates on families of subsets of a choice type, relative to a subset X; the intersection of a class of Borel families is taken in 2^X, so the empty class gives 2^X. | The printed intersection is otherwise not a family of subsets of X for the empty class. | [`is_borel_family`](../theories/BorelFamilyUP.v#L15) ([`BorelFamilyUP.v`](../theories/BorelFamilyUP.v)), [`borel_family_intersection`](../theories/BorelFamilyIntersection.v#L29), [`borel_family_powerset`](../theories/BorelFamilyIntersection.v#L14) ([`BorelFamilyIntersection.v`](../theories/BorelFamilyIntersection.v)) |
| 2.2.1 | statement correction (erratum) | Both generation criteria assume ∅ ∈ S (erratum p. 1, book p. 60 line 4). The family {∅} generated by S = ∅ is not Borel over a nonempty X. | Without the empty generator the printed criterion fails. | [`generated_closed_family_borel_criterion`](../theories/BorelGenerationCriterion.v#L18) ([`BorelGenerationCriterion.v`](../theories/BorelGenerationCriterion.v)), [`empty_only_not_borel`](../theories/EmptyGeneratedFamily.v#L43) ([`EmptyGeneratedFamily.v`](../theories/EmptyGeneratedFamily.v)) |
| 2.2.1–2.2.2 | hypothesis or convention | The closed covers of open sets, the metric generation statements and both approximation assertions of 2.2.2 are proved for pseudometric spaces. | Generalisation. | [`metric_open_countable_closed_cover`](../theories/MetricOpenClosedCover.v#L21) ([`MetricOpenClosedCover.v`](../theories/MetricOpenClosedCover.v)), [`metric_borel_generated_by_closed_closure`](../theories/MetricBorelGeneration.v#L34) ([`MetricBorelGeneration.v`](../theories/MetricBorelGeneration.v)), [`metric_borel_closed_approximation`](../theories/BorelClosedApproximation.v#L24) ([`BorelClosedApproximation.v`](../theories/BorelClosedApproximation.v)) |
| 2.2.1 | hypothesis or convention | The support is specified by closedness and the criterion that every open neighbourhood of a point of the support has positive measure; Federer's formula is a theorem. | Universal property first. | [`is_measure_support`](../theories/MeasureSupportUP.v#L20) ([`MeasureSupportUP.v`](../theories/MeasureSupportUP.v)), [`measure_support_formula`](../theories/MeasureSupport.v#L34) ([`MeasureSupport.v`](../theories/MeasureSupport.v)) |
| 2.2.3 | hypothesis or convention | The measure laws of the Borel regularisation ψ are proved without measurability of the Borel sets; that hypothesis is used only for Borel regularity and agreement on Borel sets. | Stronger formulation. | [`borel_regularisation_is_measure`](../theories/BorelRegularisationLaws.v#L23) ([`BorelRegularisationLaws.v`](../theories/BorelRegularisationLaws.v)) |
| 2.2.4 | proof route | Instead of the paired recursion on a well-ordering of Γ with card(f(I_C) ∪ g(I_C)) = 2 card(I_C), the two requests (C, b), b Boolean, are encoded by a binary first coordinate and well-ordered together on a representative of continuum cardinality, one fresh point being chosen per stage. | Avoids infinite cardinal multiplication and ordinals; assumes neither the continuum hypothesis nor regularity of the continuum. | [`full_cardinality_disjoint_selections_exist`](../theories/DisjointSelectionsConstruction.v#L25) ([`DisjointSelectionsConstruction.v`](../theories/DisjointSelectionsConstruction.v)), [`sequence_cardinal_bound_doubled`](../theories/SequenceDoubleCardinality.v#L22) ([`SequenceDoubleCardinality.v`](../theories/SequenceDoubleCardinality.v)), [`is_small_prefix_well_order`](../theories/SmallPrefixWellOrderUP.v#L18) ([`SmallPrefixWellOrderUP.v`](../theories/SmallPrefixWellOrderUP.v)) |
| 2.2.7 | hypothesis or convention | Lipschitz bounds and the least constant Lip(f) range over nonnegative M, so Lip(f) = 0 occurs; the convex-domain criterion requires M ≥ 0. | A constant map has no least positive constant; a negative M satisfies the criterion vacuously on empty or one-point domains. | [`is_least_metric_lipschitz_constant`](../theories/LipschitzUP.v#L34) ([`LipschitzUP.v`](../theories/LipschitzUP.v)), [`convex_lipschitz_limsup_criterion`](../theories/ConvexLipschitzCriterion.v#L21) ([`ConvexLipschitzCriterion.v`](../theories/ConvexLipschitzCriterion.v)), [`lipschitz_negative_bound_empty_regression`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L546) ([`MeasureExamples.v`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v)) |
| 2.2.7 | interpretation | The upper limit of dist[f(x), f(z)]/abs(x − z) is the infimum over radii of the extended-real supremum of punctured local quotients, the empty supremum being −∞; it is proved equivalent to an eventual-bound condition. | The printed upper limit is undefined at isolated points without a convention. | [`metric_local_slope_limsup`](../theories/MetricSlopeLimsup.v#L31), [`metric_slope_limsup_implies_local_bound`](../theories/MetricSlopeLimsup.v#L57) ([`MetricSlopeLimsup.v`](../theories/MetricSlopeLimsup.v)) |
| 2.2.7 | proof route | In the segment argument, sup S ∈ S is obtained from the local quantitative bound instead of continuity of f. | No mean value theorem or differentiation is used. | [`interval_local_metric_bound`](../theories/IntervalLocalBound.v#L22) ([`IntervalLocalBound.v`](../theories/IntervalLocalBound.v)) |
| 2.2.8 | hypothesis or convention | Completeness is convergence of every proper Cauchy filter to an existentially given limit. | Avoids a chosen default point, and hence an inhabitedness premise. | [`is_complete_metric_space`](../theories/CompleteMetricUP.v#L17) ([`CompleteMetricUP.v`](../theories/CompleteMetricUP.v)) |
| 2.2.8–2.2.9 | proof route | The local Lipschitz estimates compare the weight of the first different coordinate with the diameter of the common-prefix set, instead of choosing k with 2^(−k−2) ≤ dist(m, n) < 2^(−k−1). | The same estimate without the dyadic-annulus step. | [`positive_sequence_first_difference`](../theories/SequenceFirstDifference.v#L20) ([`SequenceFirstDifference.v`](../theories/SequenceFirstDifference.v)), [`closed_cover_map_locally_lipschitz`](../theories/ClosedCoverMapLipschitz.v#L58) ([`ClosedCoverMapLipschitz.v`](../theories/ClosedCoverMapLipschitz.v)), [`open_embedding_map_locally_lipschitz`](../theories/OpenEmbeddingMapLipschitz.v#L45) ([`OpenEmbeddingMapLipschitz.v`](../theories/OpenEmbeddingMapLipschitz.v)) |
| 2.2.9 | interpretation | The closing remark that the maps g are "similar to" the continued-fraction homeomorphism is formalised as theorems: the continued-fraction map is a homeomorphism of the sequence space onto the irrationals in (0, 1), with unique expansions. | The printed remark makes no precise assertion. | [`continued_fraction_irrational_homeomorphism`](../theories/ContinuedFractions.v#L32), [`irrational_continued_fraction_unique_expansion`](../theories/ContinuedFractions.v#L57) ([`ContinuedFractions.v`](../theories/ContinuedFractions.v)) |
| 2.2.9 | proof route | The continued-fraction homeomorphism is proved by grouping four digits per tree level, which gives contraction by 1/16 and the open-tree laws of 2.2.9, together with a descent on rational denominators; [Bell2016](bibliography.md#bell2016) supports the comparison, while Federer's reference [PO, p. 103](bibliography.md#perron1948) was not consulted. | Reuses the embedding-tree construction; no continued-fraction theory is imported. | [`continued_fraction_homeomorphism_exists`](../theories/ContinuedFractionEmbeddingConstruction.v#L26) ([`ContinuedFractionEmbeddingConstruction.v`](../theories/ContinuedFractionEmbeddingConstruction.v)), [`continued_fraction_block_contraction`](../theories/ContinuedFractionBlocks.v#L59) ([`ContinuedFractionBlocks.v`](../theories/ContinuedFractionBlocks.v)) |
| 2.2.10 | statement correction (erratum) | Closure of F under disjoint unions is proved for a sequence S_1, S_2, S_3, … of closed sets in the cylinders N_j of 2.2.6 (erratum p. 1, book p. 66 lines 13 and 15); the book lists S_1, S_2, S_3. | The printed text lists three sets. | [`univalent_closed_projection_disjoint_sequence_union`](../theories/ClosedProjectionUnion.v#L73) ([`ClosedProjectionUnion.v`](../theories/ClosedProjectionUnion.v)) |
| 2.2.10 | statement correction (erratum) | The separation lemma concludes that ∪G and ∪H are Borel separated (erratum p. 1, book p. 67 line 2); the book prints ∪G and ∩H. | The printed intersection is wrong. | [`borel_separated_countable_unions`](../theories/BorelSeparationFamilies.v#L17) ([`BorelSeparationFamilies.v`](../theories/BorelSeparationFamilies.v)) |
| 2.2.10 | proof route | For S = X the open set is represented by X times a singleton, separately from the equation t·dist(x, X ∼ S) = 1. | The distance to the empty set has no finite value. | [`open_set_univalent_closed_projection`](../theories/OpenSetClosedProjection.v#L19) ([`OpenSetClosedProjection.v`](../theories/OpenSetClosedProjection.v)), [`empty_set_has_no_real_distance_example`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L697) ([`MeasureExamples.v`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v)) |
| 2.2.10 | hypothesis or convention | The univalent map of a closed subset of N onto R is extended continuously to all of N; countable unions of Suslin sets are proved without nonemptiness of the family, which is kept for intersections. | Stronger formulations. | [`closed_real_sequence_parametrisation`](../theories/ClosedRealParametrisation.v#L16) ([`ClosedRealParametrisation.v`](../theories/ClosedRealParametrisation.v)), [`suslin_countable_union`](../theories/SuslinFamilyClosure.v#L16), [`suslin_countable_nonempty_intersection`](../theories/SuslinFamilyClosure.v#L32) ([`SuslinFamilyClosure.v`](../theories/SuslinFamilyClosure.v)) |
| 2.2.10(1) | proof route | The denial of (1) is turned into nested inseparable closed branches by refining only the chosen closed sets through the small closed covers of 2.2.8, instead of the full tree E(s_1, …, s_k). | Avoids storing unused branches. | [`is_inseparable_closed_branches`](../theories/InseparableClosedBranchesUP.v#L23) ([`InseparableClosedBranchesUP.v`](../theories/InseparableClosedBranchesUP.v)), [`closed_image_small_inseparable_refinement`](../theories/InseparableClosedBranchesConstruction.v#L22) ([`InseparableClosedBranchesConstruction.v`](../theories/InseparableClosedBranchesConstruction.v)) |
| 2.2.11 | hypothesis or convention | The countable base U(1), U(2), … in the coding of closed sets is normalised to contain the empty set; the public theorems accept any enumerated base. | Otherwise the whole space Z is not a slice of the printed formula. | [`is_countable_open_base`](../theories/CountableOpenBaseUP.v#L20) ([`CountableOpenBaseUP.v`](../theories/CountableOpenBaseUP.v)), [`countable_base_universal_closed_set`](../theories/UniversalDescriptiveSets.v#L19) ([`UniversalDescriptiveSets.v`](../theories/UniversalDescriptiveSets.v)), [`universal_closed_whole_slice_example`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L754) ([`MeasureExamples.v`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v)) |
| 2.2.12 | proof route | The inclusion C ⊂ p[Z_0 ∩ (X × K)] uses a finite cover of the compact box K by open prefix cylinders, instead of choosing i with dist(K, N ∼ W) > 2^(−i). | Finite-prefix form of the positive-distance argument. | [`compact_box_prefix_neighborhood`](../theories/CompactBoxNeighborhoods.v#L21) ([`CompactBoxNeighborhoods.v`](../theories/CompactBoxNeighborhoods.v)), [`pruned_projection_limit_compact_fiber`](../theories/PruningClosedProjectionLimit.v#L36) ([`PruningClosedProjectionLimit.v`](../theories/PruningClosedProjectionLimit.v)) |
| 2.2.15 | proof route | Characteristic functions of nonempty closed sets A are approximated by max(0, 1 − n·dist(x, A)) instead of 2^(−n·dist(x, A)); the empty closed set is treated separately. | Avoids developing the exponential for this step. | [`closed_distance_cutoff`](../theories/ClosedSetContinuousCutoffs.v#L24) ([`ClosedSetContinuousCutoffs.v`](../theories/ClosedSetContinuousCutoffs.v)), [`closed_characteristic_baire`](../theories/ClosedCharacteristicBaire.v#L22) ([`ClosedCharacteristicBaire.v`](../theories/ClosedCharacteristicBaire.v)) |
| 2.2.15 | statement correction (local) | The final criterion on the target is stated for nonempty separable targets, and in general as "Y is empty or Y has the finite-curve property". Every map into an empty target is Baire, whereas the printed condition, applied to the empty finite set, demands a curve from R into the target. | The printed equivalence fails for an empty target; no erratum entry. | [`borel_baire_target_criterion`](../theories/BaireTargetCriterion.v#L17), [`borel_baire_target_criterion_with_empty`](../theories/BaireTargetCriterion.v#L33) ([`BaireTargetCriterion.v`](../theories/BaireTargetCriterion.v)), [`empty_target_borel_baire_counterexample`](../theories/BaireEmptyTargetBoundary.v#L56) ([`BaireEmptyTargetBoundary.v`](../theories/BaireEmptyTargetBoundary.v)) |
| 2.2.16 | proof route | The open null cover of X ∼ spt φ is indexed by the dense set times the natural radius indices, an Ulam set by 2.1.6(1), instead of a base of cardinal α with card H ≤ α. | Avoids a cardinal equality that fails for finite dense sets, and infinite cardinal arithmetic. | [`ulam_set_times_naturals`](../theories/UlamCountableProduct.v#L19) ([`UlamCountableProduct.v`](../theories/UlamCountableProduct.v)), [`is_ulam_open_null_cover`](../theories/DenseUlamSupportCoverUP.v#L15) ([`DenseUlamSupportCoverUP.v`](../theories/DenseUlamSupportCoverUP.v)), [`finite_measure_ulam_dense_support_complement_null`](../theories/UlamMeasureSupport.v#L20) ([`UlamMeasureSupport.v`](../theories/UlamMeasureSupport.v)) |
| 2.2.17 | proof route | Outer regularity of the image measure uses W = Y ∼ f[X ∼ U] for an open U ⊃ f^(−1)(B). | The closed-map argument is simpler. | [`proper_outer_open_neighborhood`](../theories/ProperOuterNeighborhood.v#L15) ([`ProperOuterNeighborhood.v`](../theories/ProperOuterNeighborhood.v)), [`proper_continuous_map_closed`](../theories/ProperContinuousClosed.v#L16) ([`ProperContinuousClosed.v`](../theories/ProperContinuousClosed.v)), [`proper_pushforward_open_outer_bound`](../theories/ProperPushforwardOuterBound.v#L40) ([`ProperPushforwardOuterBound.v`](../theories/ProperPushforwardOuterBound.v)) |
| 2.2.17 | hypothesis or convention | Whole-domain theorems prove that the image measure is Radon without the countable compact cover of X; the theorem labelled 2.2.17 retains the printed hypothesis. | The closed-map proof does not use it. | [`proper_continuous_radon_pushforward`](../theories/RadonProperPushforward.v#L21), [`federer_2_2_17_radon_pushforward`](../theories/RadonProperPushforward.v#L51) ([`RadonProperPushforward.v`](../theories/RadonProperPushforward.v)) |

## 2.3 Measurable functions

| Item | Kind | Divergence | Reason | Rocq |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2.3.1 | hypothesis or convention | Partial functions are a separate type specified by a single-valued graph; no value outside the domain and no inhabited target are assumed. | Federer's functions may have domains smaller than X. | [`partial_function`](../theories/PartialFunctionUP.v#L15), [`partial_function_domain`](../theories/PartialFunctionUP.v#L27), [`partial_function_preimage`](../theories/PartialFunctionUP.v#L33) ([`PartialFunctionUP.v`](../theories/PartialFunctionUP.v)) |
| 2.3.1 | interpretation | In "f(x) = g(x) for φ almost all x" only points where both functions are defined with a common value count, so f is almost equal to itself only when its domain is conull. | Almost equality requires an actual common value on a conull domain. | [`measure_almost_equal`](../theories/MeasureAlmostUP.v#L33) ([`MeasureAlmostUP.v`](../theories/MeasureAlmostUP.v)), [`empty_domain_not_almost_reflexive_regression`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L1285) ([`MeasureExamples.v`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v)) |
| 2.3.2 | statement correction (local) | The remark that f can always be extended to a φ almost equal function with domain X is proved with a supplied point of the target. The empty function from a one-point null space into the empty space has no total extension. | The remark fails for an empty target; no erratum entry. | [`measurable_function_total_extension`](../theories/MeasurableTotalExtension.v#L45) ([`MeasurableTotalExtension.v`](../theories/MeasurableTotalExtension.v)), [`empty_target_extension_counterexample`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L1322) ([`MeasureExamples.v`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v)) |
| 2.3.2(5) | hypothesis or convention | The criterion for countable products keeps the hypothesis that dmn f contains φ almost all of X. | For an empty family the projection conditions are vacuous, while the empty function is not measurable when φ(X) > 0. | [`measurable_function_countable_product_iff`](../theories/MeasurableCountableProducts.v#L43) ([`MeasurableCountableProducts.v`](../theories/MeasurableCountableProducts.v)), [`empty_product_needs_conull_domain_regression`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L1337) ([`MeasureExamples.v`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v)) |
| 2.3.2 | interpretation | The lower and upper limits of a sequence of partial functions are defined on the eventual domain, where all but finitely many terms are defined . | Federer does not state their domain. | [`measurable_partial_lower_limit`](../theories/MeasurableLowerLimit.v#L17) ([`MeasurableLowerLimit.v`](../theories/MeasurableLowerLimit.v)), [`measurable_partial_upper_limit`](../theories/MeasurableUpperLimit.v#L17) ([`MeasurableUpperLimit.v`](../theories/MeasurableUpperLimit.v)) |
| 2.3.4 | hypothesis or convention | Countably measurable functions are defined for pointed topological targets. | Only the zero of the target is used. | [`is_countably_measure_measurable_function`](../theories/CountablyMeasurableUP.v#L31) ([`CountablyMeasurableUP.v`](../theories/CountablyMeasurableUP.v)) |
| 2.3.5 | interpretation | "The restriction of f to C is continuous" includes C ⊂ dmn f, as made explicit. | The restriction of a partial function must be defined on C. | [`partial_function_continuous_on`](../theories/PartialContinuityUP.v#L14) ([`PartialContinuityUP.v`](../theories/PartialContinuityUP.v)), [`lusin_borel_regular`](../theories/LusinMetric.v#L18) ([`LusinMetric.v`](../theories/LusinMetric.v)) |
| 2.3.5 | proof route | Continuity on the intersection C of the finite unions D_i is proved by an ε-neighbourhood argument from relative openness of the pieces and vanishing image oscillations, instead of continuous step functions g_i converging uniformly to f on C. | No values need be chosen on empty pieces or outside the domain. | [`is_lusin_piece_system`](../theories/LusinPiecesUP.v#L18) ([`LusinPiecesUP.v`](../theories/LusinPiecesUP.v)), [`lusin_piece_sequence_limit`](../theories/LusinClosedLimit.v#L22) ([`LusinClosedLimit.v`](../theories/LusinClosedLimit.v)) |
| 2.3.5 | proof route | In the Radon case the closed set is first obtained as in the metric case and then intersected with a compact inner approximant, instead of choosing compact pieces E_(i,j) by 2.2.5. | Avoids repeating the construction. | [`lusin_radon`](../theories/LusinRadon.v#L22) ([`LusinRadon.v`](../theories/LusinRadon.v)), [`lusin_closed_approximation`](../theories/LusinClosedApproximation.v#L21) ([`LusinClosedApproximation.v`](../theories/LusinClosedApproximation.v)) |
| 2.3.6 | statement correction (local) | The corollary that f is almost equal to a Borel map of X into Y is stated with a supplied point of Y, the empty source being treated separately. | It fails for an empty target and a nonempty null source; no erratum entry. | [`lusin_borel_version_metric`](../theories/LusinBorelVersion.v#L21), [`lusin_borel_version_radon`](../theories/LusinBorelVersion.v#L38) ([`LusinBorelVersion.v`](../theories/LusinBorelVersion.v)), [`borel_version_empty_target_counterexample`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L1657) ([`MeasureExamples.v`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v)) |
| 2.3.6 | interpretation | In the Tietze corollary the set where h(x) ≠ f(x) includes the points where f is undefined; the final sentence on the separable space Z is read relative to A, not to X. | Consistent with 2.3.1; the reading on X fails for A = ∅. | [`lusin_real_approximation_metric`](../theories/LusinRealApproximation.v#L19) ([`LusinRealApproximation.v`](../theories/LusinRealApproximation.v)), [`measurable_finite_image_support`](../theories/MeasurableFiniteImageSupport.v#L18) ([`MeasurableFiniteImageSupport.v`](../theories/MeasurableFiniteImageSupport.v)), [`restricted_image_support_is_only_relative`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L1677) ([`MeasureExamples.v`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v)) |
| 2.3.7 | interpretation | Uniform convergence on B includes that B lies in the common domain of the f_n and g . | Uniform convergence of partial functions is meaningful only where they are defined. | [`partial_function_uniformly_converges_on`](../theories/UniformPartialConvergenceUP.v#L16) ([`UniformPartialConvergenceUP.v`](../theories/UniformPartialConvergenceUP.v)), [`egoroff_measurable_partial_functions`](../theories/Egoroff.v#L17) ([`Egoroff.v`](../theories/Egoroff.v)) |
| 2.3.8 | statement correction (erratum) | The collection A(φ, Y) is a set of partial functions (erratum p. 1, book p. 78 line 7: "consider the set"); the vector-space and convex-hull statements of p. 79 are proved for its total-domain members. | Partial functions do not form a vector space. | [`is_measurable_normed_function`](../theories/MeasurableNormedFunctionUP.v#L33) ([`MeasurableNormedFunctionUP.v`](../theories/MeasurableNormedFunctionUP.v)), [`total_measurable_normed_subspace`](../theories/TotalMeasurableFunctionSpace.v#L34) ([`TotalMeasurableFunctionSpace.v`](../theories/TotalMeasurableFunctionSpace.v)), [`finite_partition_measure_ball_convex_hull`](../theories/MeasureGaugeBallConvexHull.v#L24) ([`MeasureGaugeBallConvexHull.v`](../theories/MeasureGaugeBallConvexHull.v)) |
| 2.3.8 | hypothesis or convention | The target Y is a real normed space; completeness is assumed only in 2.3.9–2.3.10. The gauge of c times the characteristic function of A is computed without measurability of A. | Neither needs the omitted hypothesis. | [`measure_gauge`](../theories/MeasureGauge.v#L35) ([`MeasureGauge.v`](../theories/MeasureGauge.v)), [`measure_gauge_scaled_characteristic`](../theories/MeasureGaugeCharacteristic.v#L58) ([`MeasureGaugeCharacteristic.v`](../theories/MeasureGaugeCharacteristic.v)) |
| 2.3.10 | proof route | Choose strictly increasing N(n), rather than merely nondecreasing indices, and use tolerance 2^(-n-2) rather than 2^(-n). | Strict increase gives a genuine subsequence even when all distances vanish. | [`is_cauchy_index_selection`](../theories/CauchyIndexSelectionUP.v#L14) ([`CauchyIndexSelectionUP.v`](../theories/CauchyIndexSelectionUP.v)), [`cauchy_measure_summable_subsequence`](../theories/MeasureCauchySubsequence.v#L22) ([`MeasureCauchySubsequence.v`](../theories/MeasureCauchySubsequence.v)), [`zero_distance_strict_subsequence_regression`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L1937) ([`MeasureExamples.v`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v)) |

## 2.4 Lebesgue integration

| Item | Kind | Divergence | Reason | Rocq |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2.4.3 | hypothesis or convention | All seven assertions hold for arbitrary partial functions, not only for functions defined φ almost everywhere. | Without a conull domain there are no step approximants and the bounds are the trivial infinities. | [`measure_upper_integral_monotone`](../theories/MeasureUpperIntegralMonotone.v#L34) ([`MeasureUpperIntegralMonotone.v`](../theories/MeasureUpperIntegralMonotone.v)), [`measure_lower_integral_le_upper`](../theories/MeasureIntegralBoundsOrder.v#L43) ([`MeasureIntegralBoundsOrder.v`](../theories/MeasureIntegralBoundsOrder.v)) |
| 2.4.4(2) | proof route | The geometric approximant is left undefined outside dmn f, and infinite values are projected to zero only inside it; Federer sets u = 0 on X ∼ P. | Keeps partial domains exact; the change affects a null set. | [`partial_geometric_approximant`](../theories/MeasureGeometricApproximant.v#L28) ([`MeasureGeometricApproximant.v`](../theories/MeasureGeometricApproximant.v)), [`geometric_approximant_lower_upper`](../theories/MeasureGeometricSandwich.v#L20) ([`MeasureGeometricSandwich.v`](../theories/MeasureGeometricSandwich.v)) |
| 2.4.4(3) | hypothesis or convention | For c ≠ 0, cf is integrable if and only if f is. | Apply the forward scaling law also with the inverse scalar. | [`measure_integral_nonzero_scale_iff`](../theories/MeasureIntegralScaleLaw.v#L117) ([`MeasureIntegralScaleLaw.v`](../theories/MeasureIntegralScaleLaw.v)) |
| 2.4.4(5) | proof route | The comparison follows from monotonicity of upper and lower integrals (2.4.3(2)) and a finite-part criterion, instead of applying (2)–(4) to g − f. | Avoids forming g − f on partial domains. | [`measurable_integral_comparison`](../theories/MeasureIntegralComparison.v#L58) ([`MeasureIntegralComparison.v`](../theories/MeasureIntegralComparison.v)), [`measurable_finite_upper_integrable`](../theories/MeasurableFiniteBoundIntegrable.v#L21) ([`MeasurableFiniteBoundIntegrable.v`](../theories/MeasurableFiniteBoundIntegrable.v)) |
| 2.4.6–2.4.9 | hypothesis or convention | Nonnegativity in Fatou's lemma, nonnegativity and monotonicity in 2.4.7, and domination and convergence in 2.4.9 need hold only almost everywhere. | Removing a common null set reduces to the printed case . | [`measure_fatou`](../theories/MeasureFatou.v#L39) ([`MeasureFatou.v`](../theories/MeasureFatou.v)), [`measure_increasing_convergence`](../theories/MeasureIncreasingConvergence.v#L56) ([`MeasureIncreasingConvergence.v`](../theories/MeasureIncreasingConvergence.v)), [`measure_bounded_convergence`](../theories/MeasureBoundedConvergence.v#L67) ([`MeasureBoundedConvergence.v`](../theories/MeasureBoundedConvergence.v)) |
| 2.4.8 | hypothesis or convention | The pointwise sum of partial functions is specified by its graph through the numerical sums of 2.1.1; it is undefined where the sum is undefined, and the empty family gives the zero function. | Federer leaves the domain of such a sum implicit. | [`is_partial_function_numerical_sum`](../theories/PartialNumericalSumUP.v#L19) ([`PartialNumericalSumUP.v`](../theories/PartialNumericalSumUP.v)), [`partial_nonnegative_sum_domain`](../theories/PartialNonnegativeSumLaws.v#L17) ([`PartialNonnegativeSumLaws.v`](../theories/PartialNonnegativeSumLaws.v)) |
| 2.4.9 | proof route | Fatou's lemma for 2h − abs(f_n − g) is used through eventual strict lower bounds of the lower limit instead of the displayed lim sup calculation. | Avoids a separate lim sup operator. | [`fatou_reflected_nonnegative_error_limit`](../theories/FatouReflectedErrorLimit.v#L21) ([`FatouReflectedErrorLimit.v`](../theories/FatouReflectedErrorLimit.v)), [`extended_lower_limit_strict_eventual`](../theories/ExtendedConvergentLowerLimit.v#L17) ([`ExtendedConvergentLowerLimit.v`](../theories/ExtendedConvergentLowerLimit.v)) |
| 2.4.10 | interpretation | The identity ∫ f dψ = ∫ f·g dφ, the splitting over a measurable set and the interval formula are equivalences of integral values: one side exists with value a if and only if the other does. | The printed equations do not say which side exists. | [`measure_density_integral_iff`](../theories/MeasureChangeOfDensity.v#L24), [`measure_density_integrable_iff`](../theories/MeasureChangeOfDensity.v#L52) ([`MeasureChangeOfDensity.v`](../theories/MeasureChangeOfDensity.v)), [`measure_integral_measurable_split`](../theories/MeasureIntegralSplitting.v#L19) ([`MeasureIntegralSplitting.v`](../theories/MeasureIntegralSplitting.v)), [`measure_interval_integral_split`](../theories/MeasureIntervalSplitting.v#L43) ([`MeasureIntervalSplitting.v`](../theories/MeasureIntervalSplitting.v)) |
| 2.4.10 | interpretation | The product f·g assigns 0 to 0·(±∞), unlike the partial product of 2.3.2. | The convention 0·∞ = 0 of 2.1.1 is needed where the density is infinite and f vanishes. | [`partial_density_product`](../theories/PartialDensityProduct.v#L16) ([`PartialDensityProduct.v`](../theories/PartialDensityProduct.v)), [`measurable_density_product`](../theories/MeasurableDensityProduct.v#L47) ([`MeasurableDensityProduct.v`](../theories/MeasurableDensityProduct.v)) |
| 2.4.10 | hypothesis or convention | The interval integral is defined on an arbitrary space with a coordinate map into the extended reals; the cases X = R and X = R̄ are instances. | One statement covers both printed cases. | [`is_measure_real_interval_integral`](../theories/MeasureIntervalIntegral.v#L33), [`is_measure_extended_interval_integral`](../theories/MeasureIntervalIntegral.v#L40) ([`MeasureIntervalIntegral.v`](../theories/MeasureIntervalIntegral.v)) |
| 2.4.11 | proof route | The zero extension of abs(f) to A is bounded pointwise by the truncation remainder plus c times the characteristic function of A, instead of splitting ∫_A abs(f) into ∫_A (abs(f) − g_n) + ∫_A g_n. | Avoids an undefined signed integral at infinite values. | [`truncation_subset_pointwise_bound`](../theories/TruncationSubsetComparison.v#L23) ([`TruncationSubsetComparison.v`](../theories/TruncationSubsetComparison.v)), [`measure_integral_absolute_continuity`](../theories/MeasureIntegralAbsoluteContinuity.v#L24) ([`MeasureIntegralAbsoluteContinuity.v`](../theories/MeasureIntegralAbsoluteContinuity.v)) |

The triangle inequality is proved on A for sums on the common domain. | Partial functions do not form a vector space. | [`total_measurable_lp_norm_seminorm`](../theories/MeasureLpNormTotal.v#L84) ([`MeasureLpNormTotal.v`](../theories/MeasureLpNormTotal.v)), [`total_measure_essential_norm_seminorm`](../theories/MeasureEssentialNormTotal.v#L31) ([`MeasureEssentialNormTotal.v`](../theories/MeasureEssentialNormTotal.v)), [`measure_lp_norm_triangle`](../theories/MeasureLpTriangle.v#L23) ([`MeasureLpTriangle.v`](../theories/MeasureLpTriangle.v)) |
| 2.4.12 | statement correction (erratum) | Density of the functions with finite image in L_p(φ, Y) is asserted only when p < ∞ or dim Y < ∞ (erratum p. 2, book p. 87 line 18). | 2.4.12 | statement correction (erratum) | The set σ^(−1)(im τ) is a φ_(1) closed subspace, and the finite-image functions form a subspace, where the book says vectorsubspace (erratum p. 2, book p. 87 line 32 and p. 88 line 3); additivity is stated for sums on the common domain. | The collection L_1(φ, Y) is not a vector space. | [`measure_bidual_integral_add`](../theories/MeasureBidualIntegral.v#L69) ([`MeasureBidualIntegral.v`](../theories/MeasureBidualIntegral.v)), [`measure_vector_integral_add`](../theories/MeasureVectorIntegralAddition.v#L19) ([`MeasureVectorIntegralAddition.v`](../theories/MeasureVectorIntegralAddition.v)) |
| 2.4.12 | statement correction (local) | The corollary of the Hahn–Banach theorem is proved only for v with σ(v) < ∞. The seminorm on R that is 0 at 0 and ∞ elsewhere has no norming functional at 1. | A real-valued functional cannot take the value ∞. | [`extended_seminorm_norming_functional`](../theories/ExtendedSeminormNorming.v#L66) ([`ExtendedSeminormNorming.v`](../theories/ExtendedSeminormNorming.v)), [`infinite_seminorm_has_no_norming_functional`](../theories/ExtendedSeminormNormingCorrection.v#L55) ([`ExtendedSeminormNormingCorrection.v`](../theories/ExtendedSeminormNormingCorrection.v)) |
| 2.4.12 | interpretation | The infimum defining φ_(∞)(f) is taken over nonnegative thresholds r. With all real thresholds the zero measure gives a negative value. | The display does not state the range of r. | [`is_measure_essential_norm`](../theories/MeasureEssentialNormUP.v#L27) ([`MeasureEssentialNormUP.v`](../theories/MeasureEssentialNormUP.v)), [`unrestricted_essential_thresholds_fail_nonnegativity`](../theories/EssentialNormThresholdCorrection.v#L23) ([`EssentialNormThresholdCorrection.v`](../theories/EssentialNormThresholdCorrection.v)) |
| 2.4.12 | hypothesis or convention | The lower semicontinuity of φ_(p) assumes a measurable limit for p < ∞ and holds for arbitrary almost convergent partial functions for p = ∞. The superlevel estimate and the gauge bound hold for every measurable f, and the superlevel estimate for every p > 0. Finite-image density for p < ∞ and the finite-image integral need no completeness of Y. | The value φ_(p)(v) is specified only for measurable v; the remaining hypotheses are not used. | [`measure_lp_norm_lower_semicontinuous`](../theories/MeasureLpNormLowerLimit.v#L26) ([`MeasureLpNormLowerLimit.v`](../theories/MeasureLpNormLowerLimit.v)), [`measure_essential_norm_lower_semicontinuous`](../theories/MeasureEssentialNormLowerLimit.v#L20) ([`MeasureEssentialNormLowerLimit.v`](../theories/MeasureEssentialNormLowerLimit.v)), [`measure_norm_power_superlevel_bound`](../theories/MeasureNormPowerMarkov.v#L22) ([`MeasureNormPowerMarkov.v`](../theories/MeasureNormPowerMarkov.v)), [`measure_gauge_le_lp_norm_power`](../theories/MeasureLpGaugeBound.v#L63) ([`MeasureLpGaugeBound.v`](../theories/MeasureLpGaugeBound.v)) |
| 2.4.12 | proof route | Reversed proof order: the integral is first built as a limit of integrals of finite-image approximants, using the L_1 Cauchy estimate and completeness of Y; σ is then τ composed with the integral, and σ^(−1)(im τ) = L_1 follows.
