| 2.4.13 | hypothesis or convention | The inequality and its equality criterion are also proved for nonnegative bases. | The zero-base boundary cases are proved explicitly. | [`weighted_arithmetic_geometric_mean`](../theories/WeightedArithmeticGeometricMean.v#L44), [`weighted_arithmetic_geometric_mean_equality`](../theories/WeightedArithmeticGeometricMean.v#L59) ([`WeightedArithmeticGeometricMean.v`](../theories/WeightedArithmeticGeometricMean.v)) |
| 2.4.14 | interpretation | The exponents are finite reals, so p, q ≥ 1 with 1/p + 1/q = 1 means 1 < p, q < ∞; the endpoint pair is treated in 2.4.16. "Almost linearly dependent" means that one signed power is almost everywhere a constant multiple of the other, in either order. | The reciprocal identity forces p, q > 1; Federer does not explain the phrase . | [`holder_inequality_and_equality`](../theories/MeasureHolder.v#L24) ([`MeasureHolder.v`](../theories/MeasureHolder.v)), [`is_almost_signed_power_dependent`](../theories/MeasureSignedPowerDependenceUP.v#L18) ([`MeasureSignedPowerDependenceUP.v`](../theories/MeasureSignedPowerDependenceUP.v)) |
| 2.4.15 | hypothesis or convention | Minkowski's inequality and its equality criteria hold for normed targets, the bracketed criterion for inner-product spaces, without completeness. | Neither proof uses completeness. | [`minkowski_inequality`](../theories/MeasureMinkowski.v#L22) ([`MeasureMinkowski.v`](../theories/MeasureMinkowski.v)), [`minkowski_equality_iff`](../theories/MeasureMinkowskiEquality.v#L61) ([`MeasureMinkowskiEquality.v`](../theories/MeasureMinkowskiEquality.v)), [`minkowski_inner_equality_iff`](../theories/MeasureMinkowskiInnerEquality.v#L47) ([`MeasureMinkowskiInnerEquality.v`](../theories/MeasureMinkowskiInnerEquality.v)) |
| 2.4.15 | interpretation | "Almost positively proportional" means f = cg or g = cf almost everywhere on the common domain for one coefficient c ≥ 0 chosen before the quantifier. | Federer does not explain the phrase . | [`measure_almost_positively_proportional`](../theories/PositiveProportionalityUP.v#L22) ([`PositiveProportionalityUP.v`](../theories/PositiveProportionalityUP.v)) |
| 2.4.16 | statement correction (erratum) | The tests range over u ∈ L_q(φ) with φ_(q)(u) ≤ 1 (erratum p. 2, book p. 89 line 19), not φ_(q)(u) = 1. | The zero-norm case needs a test of norm zero. | [`is_finite_lp_unit_test`](../theories/MeasureDualTestUP.v#L20) ([`MeasureDualTestUP.v`](../theories/MeasureDualTestUP.v)), [`finite_zero_dual_supremum`](../theories/MeasureFiniteZeroDualSupremum.v#L20) ([`MeasureFiniteZeroDualSupremum.v`](../theories/MeasureFiniteZeroDualSupremum.v)) |
| 2.4.16 | interpretation | The suprema range over the defined integrals of f·u; "sign u = sign f" is equality of partial sign functions, domains included. | The display leaves both points implicit. | [`finite_dual_integral_values`](../theories/MeasureDualTestUP.v#L44), [`is_sign_matching_test`](../theories/MeasureDualTestUP.v#L36) ([`MeasureDualTestUP.v`](../theories/MeasureDualTestUP.v)) |
| 2.4.16 | hypothesis or convention | For p = 1 the theorem holds for countably measurable partial functions, and sign f attains the supremum for every value of φ_(1)(f), including 0 and ∞. | Stronger formulation. | [`lone_dual_supremum`](../theories/MeasureLoneDuality.v#L48) ([`MeasureLoneDuality.v`](../theories/MeasureLoneDuality.v)) |
| 2.4.16 | proof route | Sign-preserving tests are built from a positive integrable weight that is positive on all of {f ≠ 0}, instead of Federer's partition into sets A_j of finite positive measure; for p = ∞ a normalised characteristic test is mixed with a full-sign test. | The printed partition may miss null parts of {f ≠ 0}, where the sign condition must also hold. | [`is_positive_integrable_weight`](../theories/PositiveIntegrableWeightUP.v#L18) ([`PositiveIntegrableWeightUP.v`](../theories/PositiveIntegrableWeightUP.v)), [`full_sign_unit_test_exists`](../theories/FullSignUnitTest.v#L18) ([`FullSignUnitTest.v`](../theories/FullSignUnitTest.v)), [`linfinity_full_sign_test_above`](../theories/MeasureLinfinityDuality.v#L24) ([`MeasureLinfinityDuality.v`](../theories/MeasureLinfinityDuality.v)) |
| 2.4.16 | proof route | When φ_(p)(f) = ∞ the approximants are restrictions of f to bounded pieces of finite measure, and their norms are shown unbounded by Fatou's lemma rather than by 2.4.7. | Restrictions give identical products against f. | [`is_bounded_measure_approximation`](../theories/BoundedMeasureApproximationUP.v#L19) ([`BoundedMeasureApproximationUP.v`](../theories/BoundedMeasureApproximationUP.v)), [`bounded_approximation_norm_unbounded`](../theories/BoundedApproximationNorms.v#L37) ([`BoundedApproximationNorms.v`](../theories/BoundedApproximationNorms.v)) |
| 2.4.17 | interpretation | Continuity of p ↦ φ_(p)(f) on Closure P is continuity for the extended-real order topologies on both sides; the cases P = ∅ and P = {∞} are proved separately. | The values and the closure live in the extended reals. | [`measure_exponent_norm_continuous_on_closure`](../theories/MeasureExponentContinuity.v#L21) ([`MeasureExponentContinuity.v`](../theories/MeasureExponentContinuity.v)) |
| 2.4.17 | hypothesis or convention | The final assertion is proved for s ≤ t, not only s < t. | The case s = t is trivial. | [`probability_measure_exponent_norm_monotone`](../theories/ProbabilityExponentMonotone.v#L20) ([`ProbabilityExponentMonotone.v`](../theories/ProbabilityExponentMonotone.v)) |
| 2.4.18(1) | interpretation | The integral identity includes definedness: both sides exist with the same value, or both are undefined. | The display does not treat undefined integrals. | [`measure_pushforward_integral_iff`](../theories/PushforwardIntegral.v#L21), [`measure_pushforward_integrable_iff`](../theories/PushforwardIntegral.v#L48) ([`PushforwardIntegral.v`](../theories/PushforwardIntegral.v)) |
| 2.4.18(1), (2) | hypothesis or convention | The measurability assertions hold for maps into an arbitrary topological space. | The order of the extended reals is not used. | [`pushforward_function_measurable_from_composition`](../theories/PushforwardMeasurableFunctions.v#L17) ([`PushforwardMeasurableFunctions.v`](../theories/PushforwardMeasurableFunctions.v)) |
| 2.4.19 | hypothesis or convention | The function F is total on Y, with its hypotheses only on C, and membership of the mean in C is an explicit conclusion. | Makes the implicit requirement on the mean explicit. | [`measure_jensen`](../theories/MeasureJensen.v#L82), [`measure_jensen_integral_values`](../theories/MeasureJensen.v#L53) ([`MeasureJensen.v`](../theories/MeasureJensen.v)) |
| 2.4.19 | proof route | The intermediate case needs only F bounded: the error of the C-valued finite-image approximants is dominated by the distance to a fixed point of C. | Boundedness of C is unnecessary on a finite-measure source. | [`bounded_range_jensen`](../theories/BoundedRangeJensen.v#L27) ([`BoundedRangeJensen.v`](../theories/BoundedRangeJensen.v)), [`measure_closed_range_lp_one_approximation`](../theories/MeasureClosedRangeLpApproximation.v#L28) ([`MeasureClosedRangeLpApproximation.v`](../theories/MeasureClosedRangeLpApproximation.v)) |
| 2.4.19 | proof route | The final exhaustion keeps φ and replaces g by a fixed point c of C outside A_n, instead of restricting φ to A_n and normalising by φ(A_n). | Avoids restricted, renormalised measures. | [`jensen_sublevel_approximation`](../theories/JensenSublevelApproximation.v#L36) ([`JensenSublevelApproximation.v`](../theories/JensenSublevelApproximation.v)), [`finite_integral_jensen`](../theories/FiniteIntegralJensen.v#L23) ([`FiniteIntegralJensen.v`](../theories/FiniteIntegralJensen.v)), [`total_jensen`](../theories/TotalJensen.v#L22) ([`TotalJensen.v`](../theories/TotalJensen.v)) |

| 2.5.1 | statement correction (erratum) | The maximum sup{f, g} = f + (g − inf{f, g}) belongs to L for all f, g ∈ L (erratum p. 2, book p. 91 line 28); the printed sign restriction is removed. | The difference g − inf{f, g} is ordered | [`function_lattice_maximum`](../theories/FunctionLatticeLaws.v#L30) ([`FunctionLatticeLaws.v`](../theories/FunctionLatticeLaws.v)) |

| 2.5.3 | interpretation | "We infer the same for the classes F_1, F_2" means that two measures satisfying 2.5.2 agree on F_0 and F_1, define the same class F_2 and agree on it. | The class F_2 depends on the measure through the condition φ(A_1) < ∞. | [`represented_lattice_level_families_agree`](../theories/FunctionLatticeLevelAgreement.v#L29), [`represented_lattice_level_determination`](../theories/FunctionLatticeLevelAgreement.v#L52) ([`FunctionLatticeLevelAgreement.v`](../theories/FunctionLatticeLevelAgreement.v)) |
| 2.5.3 | proof route | The F_2 hull of equal measure comes from F_1 outer approximations with errors 1/(n + 1) and finite intersections, instead of the double limit through B_(i,j) and V_i | order bounds avoid the double-limit extraction. | [`daniell_cover_finite_hull`](../theories/DaniellFiniteHulls.v#L20) ([`DaniellFiniteHulls.v`](../theories/DaniellFiniteHulls.v)), [`daniell_inner_difference_approximation`](../theories/DaniellInnerDifferenceApproximation.v#L21) ([`DaniellInnerDifferenceApproximation.v`](../theories/DaniellInnerDifferenceApproximation.v)) |
| 2.5.3 | hypothesis or convention | Approximation by L^+ functions in integral error is proved for summable partial functions nonnegative only almost everywhere. | Generalises the printed hypothesis. | [`daniell_nonnegative_summable_lattice_density`](../theories/DaniellPositiveL1Density.v#L29) ([`DaniellPositiveL1Density.v`](../theories/DaniellPositiveL1Density.v)) |
| 2.5.4 | hypothesis or convention | The functionals λ_1 and λ_2 are defined by the Riemann integral of 2.5.17, and L^1 is any K(R) regular measure representing it; the identification with the printed integrals is proved separately. | Lebesgue measure is characterised by the uniqueness of 2.5.3, not by a construction. | [`plane_section_integral`](../theories/PlaneRegularMeasureExamples.v#L104) ([`PlaneRegularMeasureExamples.v`](../theories/PlaneRegularMeasureExamples.v)), [`plane_section_integral_lebesgue`](../theories/PlaneSectionLebesgue.v#L30) ([`PlaneSectionLebesgue.v`](../theories/PlaneSectionLebesgue.v)) |
| 2.5.7 | statement correction (erratum) | The functional μ is defined on the total-domain space L_p(φ) ∩ {f : dmn f = X} (erratum p. 2, book p. 98 line 10), and M is the supremum over φ_(p)(f) ≤ 1 (book p. 98 line 11). | The seminorms live on total functions; the unit sphere may be empty. | [`total_scalar_lp`](../theories/TotalScalarLpUP.v#L34) ([`TotalScalarLpUP.v`](../theories/TotalScalarLpUP.v)), [`lp_linear_functional_representation`](../theories/LpLinearFunctionalRepresentation.v#L46) ([`LpLinearFunctionalRepresentation.v`](../theories/LpLinearFunctionalRepresentation.v)), [`function_operator_seminorm`](../theories/FunctionOperatorSeminorm.v#L19) ([`FunctionOperatorSeminorm.v`](../theories/FunctionOperatorSeminorm.v)) |
| 2.5.7 | statement correction (erratum) | In the proof, the residual Daniell integral and its separation by 2.5.5 are taken on L^+ (erratum p. 2, book p. 99 lines 13, 14 and 17). | The variation μ^+ is defined only on L^+. | [`lp_density_residual_daniell`](../theories/LpDensityResidual.v#L28), [`lp_density_residual_separation`](../theories/LpDensityResidual.v#L47) ([`LpDensityResidual.v`](../theories/LpDensityResidual.v)) |

| 2.5.9 | statement correction (erratum) | The extension μ_1 is defined on the total space of φ summable functions, using density of L^+ in its positive cone instead of φ_(1) density of L in L_1(φ) (erratum p. 2, book p. 100 lines 21–24). | A lattice of nonnegative functions cannot be dense in L_1(φ). | [`dominated_lattice_integral_representation`](../theories/DominatedLatticeRepresentation.v#L20) ([`DominatedLatticeRepresentation.v`](../theories/DominatedLatticeRepresentation.v)), [`regular_positive_lattice_lone_dense`](../theories/RegularLatticeLoneDensity.v#L34) ([`RegularLatticeLoneDensity.v`](../theories/RegularLatticeLoneDensity.v)) |
| 2.5.9 | hypothesis or convention | Almost uniqueness of k holds among all φ measurable densities, without bounds on the competitors; uniqueness of μ_1 holds for an arbitrary functional bounded by an L regular F. | Stronger formulations. | [`real_lattice_density_almost_unique`](../theories/RealLatticeDensityUniqueness.v#L24) ([`RealLatticeDensityUniqueness.v`](../theories/RealLatticeDensityUniqueness.v)), [`dominated_lone_extension_unique`](../theories/DominatedLoneExtensionUniqueness.v#L45) ([`DominatedLoneExtensionUniqueness.v`](../theories/DominatedLoneExtensionUniqueness.v)) |
| 2.5.9 | proof route | Almost uniqueness is proved directly by clipped L^+ approximations, dominated convergence and the L^+ series cover of 2.5.3, not through 2.5.7(ii). | Unbounded competitors fall outside the L_∞ duality. | [`real_lattice_density_almost_unique`](../theories/RealLatticeDensityUniqueness.v#L24) ([`RealLatticeDensityUniqueness.v`](../theories/RealLatticeDensityUniqueness.v)) |
| 2.5.10 | interpretation | In the question on p. 100 the φ measurable k may be extended-real valued, and both readings of f·k (partial product, and 0·∞ = 0) are treated; each such density reduces to a real one. The negative answer of 2.5.11 covers both readings. | The product 0·∞ is undefined unless a convention is indicated, and the question indicates none. | [`is_extended_lattice_density`](../theories/ExtendedLatticeDensity.v#L32), [`extended_lattice_density_real`](../theories/ExtendedLatticeDensity.v#L86) ([`ExtendedLatticeDensity.v`](../theories/ExtendedLatticeDensity.v)), [`example_no_extended_density`](../theories/CardinalProductUnconditional.v#L42) ([`CardinalProductUnconditional.v`](../theories/CardinalProductUnconditional.v)) |
