| 2.5.10 | proof route | In the Radon paragraph the open V ⊃ C with φ(V) < ∞ comes from Radon outer approximation; the printed proof takes V with compact closure. | Only φ(V) < ∞ is used. | [`radon_compact_meeting_countable`](../theories/RadonCompactMeetingFamily.v#L24) ([`RadonCompactMeetingFamily.v`](../theories/RadonCompactMeetingFamily.v)) |
| 2.5.10 | hypothesis or convention | The continuum hypothesis is the proposition [`continuum_hypothesis`](../theories/ContinuumHypothesisUP.v#L17), an explicit premise of each dependent theorem and never an axiom. | It is not provable, and the audit admits only three axioms. | [`continuum_hypothesis`](../theories/ContinuumHypothesisUP.v#L17) ([`ContinuumHypothesisUP.v`](../theories/ContinuumHypothesisUP.v)), [`continuum_borel_lone_functional_representation`](../theories/ContinuumBorelRepresentation.v#L18) ([`ContinuumBorelRepresentation.v`](../theories/ContinuumBorelRepresentation.v)) |
| 2.5.10 | hypothesis or convention | In the geometric example μ is integration against a Carathéodory measure built from the cost L^1(p[S]); the fibre-sum formula is a theorem, with the signed fibre sum undefined where both parts are infinite. Of the results cited from 2.6 and 2.10 only the planar one-dimensional cases are formalised. | Linearity and the bound come from the integral laws; ∞ − ∞ is not given a value. | [`plane_projection_measure`](../theories/PlaneProjectionMeasure.v#L32) ([`PlaneProjectionMeasure.v`](../theories/PlaneProjectionMeasure.v)), [`plane_projection_functional_fibre_formula`](../theories/PlaneProjectionFormula.v#L54) ([`PlaneProjectionFormula.v`](../theories/PlaneProjectionFormula.v)), [`fibre_numerical_integrand`](../theories/FibreNumericalIntegrand.v#L20) ([`FibreNumericalIntegrand.v`](../theories/FibreNumericalIntegrand.v)), [`hausdorff_length`](../theories/HausdorffLength.v#L15) ([`HausdorffLength.v`](../theories/HausdorffLength.v)) |
| 2.5.10 | interpretation | The claim that k cannot be L^2 measurable is proved for every H^1 measurable density representing μ, without the continuum hypothesis; that hypothesis is used only for the existence of such a density. | Federer leaves the claim to the reader; the obstruction does not depend on it. | [`plane_projection_density_not_lebesgue_measurable`](../theories/PlaneDensityNonmeasurability.v#L29), [`federer_2_5_10_geometric_example`](../theories/PlaneDensityNonmeasurability.v#L86) ([`PlaneDensityNonmeasurability.v`](../theories/PlaneDensityNonmeasurability.v)) |
| 2.5.11 | hypothesis or convention | The sets Y_1, Y_2 are choice types with the successor predicates for ℵ_1 and ℵ_2, comparison meaning existence of an injection; their existence is proved separately, which gives an unconditional theorem. The measures β_j assume that countable unions of small sets are small. | No ordinals are constructed; Federer uses this property of the bound tacitly. | [`is_cardinal_successor`](../theories/CardinalSuccessorUP.v#L19) ([`CardinalSuccessorUP.v`](../theories/CardinalSuccessorUP.v)), [`federer_2_5_10_negative_answer_unconditional`](../theories/CardinalProductUnconditional.v#L69) ([`CardinalProductUnconditional.v`](../theories/CardinalProductUnconditional.v)), [`is_sigma_small_bound`](../theories/CardinalMeasureUP.v#L26) ([`CardinalMeasureUP.v`](../theories/CardinalMeasureUP.v)) |
| 2.5.11 | statement correction (erratum) | The lattice is the intersection of the total-domain spaces L̃_1(α_1 × β_2) and L̃_1(β_1 × α_2) (erratum p. 2, book p. 102 line 8); L_1(φ) = L is stated for the total space (book p. 102 line 12), and the negative answer concerns μ on the total space (book p. 98 line 10). | Federer's corrections. | [`example_lattice`](../theories/CardinalProductExample.v#L83) ([`CardinalProductExample.v`](../theories/CardinalProductExample.v)), [`example_lone_identity`](../theories/CardinalProductLoneIdentity.v#L212) ([`CardinalProductLoneIdentity.v`](../theories/CardinalProductLoneIdentity.v)), [`federer_2_5_10_negative_answer_lone`](../theories/CardinalProductLoneFunctional.v#L159) ([`CardinalProductLoneFunctional.v`](../theories/CardinalProductLoneFunctional.v)) |
| 2.5.11 | proof route | The line integrals of k are obtained without L_1(φ) = L: φ is identified on the lines as images of β_2 and β_1 by the envelope estimates of 2.5.2, the maximality of 2.6.1 and the level estimate of 2.5.3. | The identity L_1(φ) = L is proved separately. | [`phi_restrict_vertical`](../theories/CardinalProductStrips.v#L314), [`phi_restrict_horizontal`](../theories/CardinalProductStrips.v#L330) ([`CardinalProductStrips.v`](../theories/CardinalProductStrips.v)), [`example_no_density`](../theories/CardinalProductCounterexample.v#L247) ([`CardinalProductCounterexample.v`](../theories/CardinalProductCounterexample.v)) |
| 2.5.12 | hypothesis or convention | The weak topology of E* is the subspace topology of the product topology on all real functions on E; the countable K ⊂ L^+ is a sequence. | Modelling choices that generate the printed topology and cover. | [`weak_dual_space`](../theories/VectorDaniellHypotheses.v#L65), [`has_lattice_unit_series`](../theories/VectorDaniellHypotheses.v#L102) ([`VectorDaniellHypotheses.v`](../theories/VectorDaniellHypotheses.v)) |
| 2.5.12 | hypothesis or convention | The density k is total, with values in the dual unit ball at every point; almost uniqueness holds among all weakly measurable densities, and the pairing conclusion includes a bound by ∫ ν ∘ η dφ. For dim E = 0 the conclusion forces φ(X) = 0. | Representative convention and stronger formulations; sup ∅ = 0. | [`is_normalised_vector_daniell_density`](../theories/VectorDaniellNormalisationUP.v#L21) ([`VectorDaniellNormalisationUP.v`](../theories/VectorDaniellNormalisationUP.v)), [`vector_daniell_density_almost_unique`](../theories/VectorDaniellUniqueness.v#L76) ([`VectorDaniellUniqueness.v`](../theories/VectorDaniellUniqueness.v)), [`vector_daniell_pairing_consequences`](../theories/VectorDaniellRepresentation.v#L117) ([`VectorDaniellRepresentation.v`](../theories/VectorDaniellRepresentation.v)) |
| 2.5.12 | proof route | Measurability of ⟨η, k⟩ is proved without continuity of the pairing on E × U* and 2.3.2(4), (5), by writing it as a countable supremum over a dense set D. | Avoids a product topology on E × U*. | [`measurable_unit_dual_pairing`](../theories/MeasurableDualPairing.v#L80) ([`MeasurableDualPairing.v`](../theories/MeasurableDualPairing.v)), [`vector_density_pairing_measurable`](../theories/VectorDaniellPairingConclusion.v#L68) ([`VectorDaniellPairingConclusion.v`](../theories/VectorDaniellPairingConclusion.v)) |
| 2.5.12 | proof route | The identity G = Q is proved by one L_1 approximation of w by finite sums, instead of the finite-support step followed by truncation with inf{ν ∘ w, 1/n}; the equation ν* ∘ k = 1 follows from 2.5.9 uniqueness rather than by contradiction. | Combines the printed steps. | [`daniell_vector_lone_density`](../theories/DaniellVectorLoneDensity.v#L28) ([`DaniellVectorLoneDensity.v`](../theories/DaniellVectorLoneDensity.v)), [`represented_vector_density_unit_norm`](../theories/VectorDaniellUnitNorm.v#L60) ([`VectorDaniellUnitNorm.v`](../theories/VectorDaniellUnitNorm.v)) |
| 2.5.13 | proof route | The continuity hypothesis of 2.5.5 is verified for arbitrary increasing sequences in K(X) with a cutoff on the support of g − h_0, instead of g, h_n ∈ L^+ with a cutoff on spt g. | The hypothesis of 2.5.5 concerns sequences in L | [`compact_support_increasing_functional_limit`](../theories/CompactSupportDaniellContinuity.v#L22) ([`CompactSupportDaniellContinuity.v`](../theories/CompactSupportDaniellContinuity.v)) |
| 2.5.14 | hypothesis or convention | Finiteness on compact sets and measurability of the strict levels are proved for every measure representing λ, not only the K(X) regular one; the scalar densities for Φ are bounded measurable rather than in L_∞(Φ); the Radon density has unit norm at every point, and local uniqueness holds among all densities. | Stronger formulations; L_∞ would add a countable-support condition. | [`compact_support_representing_measure_compact_finite`](../theories/CompactSupportMeasureFinite.v#L21) ([`CompactSupportMeasureFinite.v`](../theories/CompactSupportMeasureFinite.v)), [`radon_vector_component_density`](../theories/RadonVectorComponentDensity.v#L24) ([`RadonVectorComponentDensity.v`](../theories/RadonVectorComponentDensity.v)), [`radon_vector_representation_locally_unique`](../theories/RadonVectorRepresentation.v#L73) ([`RadonVectorRepresentation.v`](../theories/RadonVectorRepresentation.v)) |
| 2.5.14 | interpretation | "Hypothesis (5) is here redundant" refers to its continuity clause only; linearity of T and finiteness of λ(f) are kept. | Dropping them would admit nonlinear or unbounded functionals. | [`is_order_bounded_vector_functional`](../theories/OrderBoundedVectorFunctionalUP.v#L19) ([`OrderBoundedVectorFunctionalUP.v`](../theories/OrderBoundedVectorFunctionalUP.v)), [`radon_vector_representation`](../theories/RadonVectorRepresentation.v#L26) ([`RadonVectorRepresentation.v`](../theories/RadonVectorRepresentation.v)) |
| 2.5.14 | interpretation | "Thus φ may fail to be a Radon measure" is formalised existentially and as the statement that every K(X) regular measure representing evaluation on the space of 2.5.15 is not Radon. "Locally almost" refers to sets meeting every set of finite measure in a null set. | Precise meanings for the modal phrase and the local qualification. | [`regular_measure_may_fail_radon`](../theories/UncountableProductNotRadon.v#L50), [`evaluation_regular_measure_not_radon`](../theories/UncountableProductNotRadon.v#L29) ([`UncountableProductNotRadon.v`](../theories/UncountableProductNotRadon.v)), [`measure_locally_null`](../theories/LocallyNullMeasureUP.v#L17) ([`LocallyNullMeasureUP.v`](../theories/LocallyNullMeasureUP.v)) |
| 2.5.14 | proof route | Agreement of Φ and φ on F_1 is proved from the compact closed-level approximants of 2.5.3 and localised cutoffs, instead of the truncation inequality and the right limits; the uses of K(X) regularity in the modified 2.5.12 are replaced by compact inner and open outer approximation of Φ. | The measure Φ is Radon but is not assumed K(X) regular. | [`compact_open_envelope_increasing_level_value`](../theories/CompactOpenContentLevelAgreement.v#L58) ([`CompactOpenContentLevelAgreement.v`](../theories/CompactOpenContentLevelAgreement.v)), [`compact_support_radon_representation`](../theories/CompactSupportRadonRepresentation.v#L21) ([`CompactSupportRadonRepresentation.v`](../theories/CompactSupportRadonRepresentation.v)), [`radon_compact_characteristic_approximable`](../theories/RadonCompactCharacteristicApproximation.v#L28) ([`RadonCompactCharacteristicApproximation.v`](../theories/RadonCompactCharacteristicApproximation.v)) |
| 2.5.15 | proof route | Every f ∈ K(X) depends only on countably many coordinates, by compactness of X and the finite-coordinate neighbourhoods of the product topology; Federer cites the Weierstrass–Stone theorem. | Avoids the Weierstrass–Stone theorem. | [`compact_product_continuous_countably_determined`](../theories/ProductCoordinateNeighborhoods.v#L117) ([`ProductCoordinateNeighborhoods.v`](../theories/ProductCoordinateNeighborhoods.v)) |
| 2.5.16 | hypothesis or convention | The variation, arc length, factor G and separability of im g are formalised for an arbitrary metric space Y; completeness, which Federer assumes from the outset, is used only for one-sided limits, jumps and the extension H. The norm of the space E is 0 when Y is empty. | Completeness is needed only for limits of g. | [`curve_variation`](../theories/CurveVariationUP.v#L106) ([`CurveVariationUP.v`](../theories/CurveVariationUP.v)), [`curve_left_limit_exists`](../theories/CurveOneSidedLimits.v#L115) ([`CurveOneSidedLimits.v`](../theories/CurveOneSidedLimits.v)), [`is_bounded_continuous_space`](../theories/BoundedContinuousSpaceUP.v#L23) ([`BoundedContinuousSpaceUP.v`](../theories/BoundedContinuousSpaceUP.v)), [`is_uniform_function_norm`](../theories/UniformFunctionNormUP.v#L17) ([`UniformFunctionNormUP.v`](../theories/UniformFunctionNormUP.v)) |
| 2.5.16 | proof route | The extension H is obtained from a general lemma extending a Lipschitz map on a set of reals affinely across the gaps; the printed formulas then characterise H instead of defining it. | One construction serves every set of reals. | [`gap_interpolation_exists`](../theories/CurveBanachExtensionInterpolation.v#L550) ([`CurveBanachExtensionInterpolation.v`](../theories/CurveBanachExtensionInterpolation.v)), [`curve_banach_extension_exists`](../theories/CurveBanachExtension.v#L213) ([`CurveBanachExtension.v`](../theories/CurveBanachExtension.v)) |
| 2.5.16 | interpretation | The remark that the Banach space assumption "is not as restrictive as it may seem" becomes a theorem: for every metric Y, F ∘ g has the same variation and arc length as g and has an extension H. | The remark makes no precise assertion. | [`curve_metric_banach_reduction`](../theories/CurveMetricBanachReduction.v#L117) ([`CurveMetricBanachReduction.v`](../theories/CurveMetricBanachReduction.v)) |
| 2.5.17 | hypothesis or convention | The integral is defined for every continuous f as the limit over uniform tagged partitions; Federer's limit as the mesh tends to zero is a theorem. The value μ(f) is the integral over a symmetric segment, and the printed defining property is a theorem. | A sequential limit gives a total definition. | [`stieltjes_integral`](../theories/StieltjesIntegral.v#L227), [`stieltjes_integral_limit`](../theories/StieltjesIntegral.v#L265) ([`StieltjesIntegral.v`](../theories/StieltjesIntegral.v)), [`induced_daniell`](../theories/StieltjesDaniellIntegral.v#L97), [`induced_daniell_value`](../theories/StieltjesDaniellIntegral.v#L144) ([`StieltjesDaniellIntegral.v`](../theories/StieltjesDaniellIntegral.v)) |
| 2.5.17 | interpretation | "The measures induced by g" are the K(R) regular measures representing μ^+ and μ^−, specified by [`is_induced_measure_pair`](../theories/StieltjesImageMeasures.v#L37) and proved unique; every printed consequence holds for every such pair. | The printed text names measures that only an existence theorem supplies. | [`is_induced_measure_pair`](../theories/StieltjesImageMeasures.v#L37) ([`StieltjesImageMeasures.v`](../theories/StieltjesImageMeasures.v)), [`induced_measure_pair_unique`](../theories/StieltjesInducedPairLaws.v#L64) ([`StieltjesInducedPairLaws.v`](../theories/StieltjesInducedPairLaws.v)) |
| 2.5.18(1) | hypothesis or convention | Integration by parts assumes that f and g have finite variation on every segment. | Both integrals are those of 2.5.17. | [`stieltjes_integration_by_parts`](../theories/StieltjesIntegrationByParts.v#L117) ([`StieltjesIntegrationByParts.v`](../theories/StieltjesIntegrationByParts.v)) |
| 2.5.18(2) | hypothesis or convention | The substitution formula uses "g increasing" only as nondecreasing. | Only the order of the image partition is used. | [`stieltjes_substitution`](../theories/StieltjesSubstitution.v#L150) ([`StieltjesSubstitution.v`](../theories/StieltjesSubstitution.v)) |
| 2.5.18(2) | interpretation | With only the variation of h on [g(a), g(b)] finite, the integral is read through h composed with the retraction onto [g(a), g(b)]. | The segment integral depends only on the values there . | [`stieltjes_substitution_segment`](../theories/StieltjesSubstitution.v#L205), [`segment_retraction`](../theories/StieltjesSubstitution.v#L186) ([`StieltjesSubstitution.v`](../theories/StieltjesSubstitution.v)) |
| 2.5.18(2) | statement correction (local) | The sentence "g_# maps the measures induced by h ∘ g onto the measures induced by h" assumes im g = R. For g(x) = x/(1 + abs(x)) and h(x) = x the image measure vanishes on {t : 2 ≤ t ≤ 3}, while L^1 gives 1. | The proved counterexample verifies the need for surjectivity. | [`stieltjes_image_induced_measures`](../theories/StieltjesImageMeasures.v#L293) ([`StieltjesImageMeasures.v`](../theories/StieltjesImageMeasures.v)), [`stieltjes_image_requires_onto`](../theories/StieltjesImageSurjectivity.v#L89), [`stieltjes_image_unrestricted_false`](../theories/StieltjesImageSurjectivity.v#L120) ([`StieltjesImageSurjectivity.v`](../theories/StieltjesImageSurjectivity.v)) |
| 2.5.18(3) | proof route | The formula ∫ (f ∘ u) dφ = ∫_a^b f dg is proved directly: each Riemann–Stieltjes sum is the integral of a simple function close to f ∘ u. Federer infers it from the Borel agreement of u_#(φ) and ψ^+ and 2.4.18. | Uses only the definition of 2.5.17. | [`distribution_function_integral`](../theories/StieltjesDistributionFunction.v#L372) ([`StieltjesDistributionFunction.v`](../theories/StieltjesDistributionFunction.v)) |
| 2.5.18(3) | proof route | The Borel agreement is proved on half-open intervals with end points at continuity points of g, by a general agreement lemma; Federer argues from the open-interval display. | Supplies the measure-theoretic step behind "Consequently". | [`half_open_agreement_borel`](../theories/HalfOpenIntervalAgreement.v#L313) ([`HalfOpenIntervalAgreement.v`](../theories/HalfOpenIntervalAgreement.v)), [`distribution_image_induced_borel`](../theories/StieltjesDistributionImage.v#L123) ([`StieltjesDistributionImage.v`](../theories/StieltjesDistributionImage.v)) |
| 2.5.19 | hypothesis or convention | Members of L* are total functionals, so two that agree on L are distinct; the weak topology is the initial topology of restriction to L. The norm on K(X) is 0 on an empty domain. | Daniell integrals are predicates on total functionals. | [`daniell_weak_cvg`](../theories/DaniellSpaceWeakTopology.v#L177) ([`DaniellSpaceWeakTopology.v`](../theories/DaniellSpaceWeakTopology.v)), [`is_uniform_function_norm`](../theories/UniformFunctionNormUP.v#L17) ([`UniformFunctionNormUP.v`](../theories/UniformFunctionNormUP.v)) |
| 2.5.19 | interpretation | "The two weak topologies coincide" is read through an identification Φ of the conjugate space with Daniell integrals, a homeomorphism up to agreement on K(X). | The topologies live on different spaces. | [`compact_space_weak_topologies_coincide`](../theories/CompactSpaceWeakDual.v#L179), [`compact_function_dual_map_weak_homeomorphism`](../theories/CompactSpaceWeakDual.v#L200) ([`CompactSpaceWeakDual.v`](../theories/CompactSpaceWeakDual.v)) |
| 2.5.20 | hypothesis or convention | Empty factors and E = 0 are allowed; the identification of E* with Daniell integrals is specified with weak continuity; the disintegration records that φ represents the variation of T, the measure of the final claim. | Covers degenerate cases and makes the tacit passage through 2.5.19 explicit. | [`compact_metric_daniell_disintegration`](../theories/DaniellDisintegration.v#L30) ([`DaniellDisintegration.v`](../theories/DaniellDisintegration.v)), [`is_compact_function_dual_identification`](../theories/CompactFunctionDualUP.v#L22) ([`CompactFunctionDualUP.v`](../theories/CompactFunctionDualUP.v)), [`is_daniell_disintegration`](../theories/DaniellDisintegrationUP.v#L56) ([`DaniellDisintegrationUP.v`](../theories/DaniellDisintegrationUP.v)) |

## 2.6 Product measures

| Item | Kind | Divergence | Reason | Rocq |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2.6.1 | hypothesis or convention | The Cartesian product is specified by Federer's closing remark, as the largest measure bounded by α(A)β(B) on measurable rectangles; the defining cover infimum, first over countable families and then over sequences, is a theorem. | Universal property first. | [`is_product_measure`](../theories/ProductMeasureUP.v#L19) ([`ProductMeasureUP.v`](../theories/ProductMeasureUP.v)), [`is_product_cover_infimum`](../theories/ProductMeasureCoverUP.v#L16) ([`ProductMeasureCoverUP.v`](../theories/ProductMeasureCoverUP.v)), [`product_measure_sequence_cover_greatest`](../theories/ProductMeasureSequenceInfimum.v#L37) ([`ProductMeasureSequenceInfimum.v`](../theories/ProductMeasureSequenceInfimum.v)) |
| 2.6.2(2) | proof route | The bound (α × β)(A × B) ≥ α(A)β(B) is proved directly from the cover infimum, and measurability of A × B by splitting covering sums of the cylinders A × Y and X × B, both before the classes P_1, P_2. Federer derives them from the hull assertion and the repeated integral. | Documented change of proof order. | [`product_rectangle_cover_cost_lower`](../theories/ProductRectangleCoverLowerBound.v#L27) ([`ProductRectangleCoverLowerBound.v`](../theories/ProductRectangleCoverLowerBound.v)), [`product_measure_rectangle_measurable`](../theories/ProductRectangleMeasurability.v#L15) ([`ProductRectangleMeasurability.v`](../theories/ProductRectangleMeasurability.v)) |
| 2.6.2(3) | proof route | For (α × β)(S) = 0, null sections and a zero repeated integral come from rectangle covers of small cost and their integral majorants, instead of a set W ∈ P_2 with p(W) = 0. | No measurability of S is needed. | [`product_null_vertical_sections`](../theories/ProductNullSections.v#L22) ([`ProductNullSections.v`](../theories/ProductNullSections.v)), [`vanishing_integral_majorants_almost_zero`](../theories/VanishingIntegralMajorants.v#L29) ([`VanishingIntegralMajorants.v`](../theories/VanishingIntegralMajorants.v)) |
| 2.6.3(1) | proof route | The diagonal is shown to have infinite product measure directly from covers, whence failure of countable measurability; it is shown measurable by a rational-cut decomposition. Federer derives the value from the unequal iterated integrals and measurability from Borel regularity. | The value is obtained from covers alone, without a converse of Fubini's theorem. | [`atomless_counting_diagonal_infinite`](../theories/CountingProductDiagonal.v#L20) ([`CountingProductDiagonal.v`](../theories/CountingProductDiagonal.v)), [`lebesgue_counting_diagonal_not_countably_measurable`](../theories/LebesgueCountingDiagonal.v#L97), [`lebesgue_counting_diagonal_measurable`](../theories/LebesgueCountingDiagonal.v#L113) ([`LebesgueCountingDiagonal.v`](../theories/LebesgueCountingDiagonal.v)) |
| 2.6.3(2) | proof route | The elementary integrals are evaluated by a uniform-primitive criterion and the Riemann–Stieltjes sums of 2.5.17 instead of the fundamental theorem 2.9.20; non-integrability uses the single slope m = 1 instead of the dependence of m(1 + m)^(−2) on m. | Section 2.9 is not formalised; one slope suffices. | [`uniform_primitive_riemann_integral`](../theories/UniformPrimitiveIntegral.v#L19) ([`UniformPrimitiveIntegral.v`](../theories/UniformPrimitiveIntegral.v)), [`rational_fubini_kernel_not_integrable`](../theories/RationalFubiniCounterexample.v#L24) ([`RationalFubiniCounterexample.v`](../theories/RationalFubiniCounterexample.v)) |
| 2.6.4 | statement correction (erratum) | The space L̃_1 of total-domain summable functions replaces L_1 in the bilinear map, the monomorphism and the integrals λ, μ, ν (erratum p. 2, book p. 118 lines 20, 21, 24 and p. 119 lines 3, 4, 6). | Federer's correction. | [`is_summable_real_space`](../theories/SummableRealSpaceUP.v#L19) ([`SummableRealSpaceUP.v`](../theories/SummableRealSpaceUP.v)), [`summable_tensor_map_injective`](../theories/SummableTensorMap.v#L86) ([`SummableTensorMap.v`](../theories/SummableTensorMap.v)) |
| 2.6.4 | statement correction (erratum) | The final assertion is ν(h) = ∫ h d(α × β) for h ∈ K(X × Y) (erratum p. 2, book p. 119 line 19), not that α × β is the K(X × Y) regular measure associated with ν. | Federer's correction. | [`daniell_product_measure_representation`](../theories/DaniellProductRepresentation.v#L25) ([`DaniellProductRepresentation.v`](../theories/DaniellProductRepresentation.v)) |
| 2.6.4 | proof route | Injectivity of L̃_1(α) ⊗ L̃_1(β) → L̃_1(α × β) is proved by a finite separation argument with evaluation points, instead of 1.1.1 and 1.1.3; density of the image passes to nonnegative functions by finite simple minorants, instead of 2.3.3 and 2.4.8. | Item 1.1.3 is not formalised; the tensor image need not be closed under minima. | [`function_tensor_map_injective`](../theories/FunctionTensorInjectivity.v#L99) ([`FunctionTensorInjectivity.v`](../theories/FunctionTensorInjectivity.v)), [`subspace_nonnegative_summable_density`](../theories/SubspacePositiveDensity.v#L24) ([`SubspacePositiveDensity.v`](../theories/SubspacePositiveDensity.v)) |
| 2.6.4 | hypothesis or convention | The corrected representation holds for any measures representing the factor functionals, without K(X), K(Y) regularity. | Only summability of the factor spaces is used. | [`daniell_product_measure_representation`](../theories/DaniellProductRepresentation.v#L25) ([`DaniellProductRepresentation.v`](../theories/DaniellProductRepresentation.v)) |
| 2.6.5 | statement correction (local) | Associativity of the product is false for arbitrary measures: Lebesgue measure twice and a third factor with infinite measure on every nonempty set give the diagonal of [0, 1] times that factor measure 0 and ∞ in the two associations. It is proved for countably measurable factors, and the independence of associations in α_1 × … × α_n likewise. | no erratum entry. | [`lebesgue_product_not_associative`](../theories/LebesgueAssociativityCounterexample.v#L79) ([`LebesgueAssociativityCounterexample.v`](../theories/LebesgueAssociativityCounterexample.v)), [`product_measure_associative_countable`](../theories/ProductMeasureAssociativity.v#L133) ([`ProductMeasureAssociativity.v`](../theories/ProductMeasureAssociativity.v)), [`finite_product_association_invariance`](../theories/FiniteMeasureAssociations.v#L92) ([`FiniteMeasureAssociations.v`](../theories/FiniteMeasureAssociations.v)) |
| 2.6.5 | hypothesis or convention | Associativity needs countable measurability only of the outer factors α and γ. | The extremal-measure comparison uses countability only in the outer factors. | [`product_measure_associative_outer_countable`](../theories/ProductMeasureAssociativity.v#L116) ([`ProductMeasureAssociativity.v`](../theories/ProductMeasureAssociativity.v)) |
| 2.6.5 | proof route | Associativity is proved by comparing extremal measures through the largest-measure property of 2.6.1 | Uses only 2.6.1 and 2.6.2(2). | [`left_triple_finite_slice_bound`](../theories/TripleProductFiniteSlices.v#L63) ([`TripleProductFiniteSlices.v`](../theories/TripleProductFiniteSlices.v)), [`product_measure_associate_le`](../theories/ProductMeasureAssociativity.v#L88) ([`ProductMeasureAssociativity.v`](../theories/ProductMeasureAssociativity.v)) |
| 2.6.5 | hypothesis or convention | Finite products are specified for one fixed left association; L^n lives on the coordinate space R^n, with R^(m+n) ≅ R^m × R^n a theorem; dimension zero is included, L^0 being the unit mass. | Independence of association holds only under the corrected hypotheses. | [`is_finite_cartesian_measure_family`](../theories/FiniteCartesianMeasureUP.v#L27) ([`FiniteCartesianMeasureUP.v`](../theories/FiniteCartesianMeasureUP.v)), [`is_euclidean_lebesgue_family`](../theories/EuclideanLebesgueUP.v#L20) ([`EuclideanLebesgueUP.v`](../theories/EuclideanLebesgueUP.v)), [`euclidean_block_measure`](../theories/EuclideanBlockMeasure.v#L99) ([`EuclideanBlockMeasure.v`](../theories/EuclideanBlockMeasure.v)), [`euclidean_lebesgue_measure`](../theories/EuclideanLebesgueDimensions.v#L21) ([`EuclideanLebesgueDimensions.v`](../theories/EuclideanLebesgueDimensions.v)) |
| 2.6.6 | hypothesis or convention | The index set J is arbitrary, possibly finite or empty, where Federer assumes J infinite. The finite products μ_a are specified by empty-product, adjoining and consistency laws instead of the method of 2.6.4, and inverse systems have bonding maps for all pairs. | Covers the boundary cases; the identification with 2.6.4 is proved afterwards. | [`is_infinite_daniell_product`](../theories/InfiniteDaniellProductUP.v#L18) ([`InfiniteDaniellProductUP.v`](../theories/InfiniteDaniellProductUP.v)), [`is_finite_coordinate_product_family`](../theories/FiniteCoordinateProductUP.v#L19) ([`FiniteCoordinateProductUP.v`](../theories/FiniteCoordinateProductUP.v)), [`finite_coordinate_inverse_system`](../theories/FiniteCoordinateInverseLimit.v#L52) ([`FiniteCoordinateInverseLimit.v`](../theories/FiniteCoordinateInverseLimit.v)) |
| 2.6.6 | interpretation | "Can be applied to arbitrary inverse limits" is a theorem for nonempty directed compact Hausdorff inverse systems with consistent normalised monotone Daniell integrals; no signed version is claimed. | An unrestricted signed version is false without a bound on the variations. | [`is_compact_inverse_limit`](../theories/CompactInverseLimitUP.v#L24) ([`CompactInverseLimitUP.v`](../theories/CompactInverseLimitUP.v)), [`inverse_limit_daniell_integral`](../theories/InverseDaniellIntegral.v#L12) ([`InverseDaniellIntegral.v`](../theories/InverseDaniellIntegral.v)) |
| 2.6.7 | hypothesis or convention | The case a = b is included; f and g are real functions satisfying the defining identities only on [a, b]; an underlying theorem assumes only measurability of the half-lines. | Harmless strengthenings; the public theorem keeps the Radon hypothesis. | [`radon_cumulative_integration_by_parts`](../theories/RadonCumulativeIntegrationByParts.v#L23) ([`RadonCumulativeIntegrationByParts.v`](../theories/RadonCumulativeIntegrationByParts.v)), [`is_cumulative_function_pair`](../theories/CumulativeFunctionPairUP.v#L18) ([`CumulativeFunctionPairUP.v`](../theories/CumulativeFunctionPairUP.v)), [`cumulative_integration_by_parts`](../theories/CumulativeIntegrationByParts.v#L61) ([`CumulativeIntegrationByParts.v`](../theories/CumulativeIntegrationByParts.v)) |

## 2.7 Invariant measures

| Item | Kind | Divergence | Reason | Rocq |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2.7.1 | interpretation | Topological groups are specified by predicates on the operations: one-sided group axioms, continuity of the multiplication on G x G and of the inversion; the two-sided laws are derived. | Federer names topological groups without axioms, and MathComp has no structure of a noncommutative infinite group. | [`is_group_laws`](../theories/TopologicalGroupUP.v#L24), [`is_topological_group`](../theories/TopologicalGroupUP.v#L39), [`is_locally_compact_hausdorff_group`](../theories/TopologicalGroupUP.v#L56) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.1 | hypothesis or convention | The standing hypotheses (G and X locally compact Hausdorff) are not part of [`is_homogeneous_space`](../theories/HomogeneousSpaceUP.v#L33); A_e = 1_X, the homeomorphisms A_g and G/G^x ~ X are proved without them, and closedness of G^x uses only that X is Hausdorff. | Each result assumes only what its proof uses. | [`is_homogeneous_space`](../theories/HomogeneousSpaceUP.v#L33), [`homogeneous_translation_homeomorphism`](../theories/HomogeneousSpaceLaws.v#L78), [`isotropy_subgroup_closed`](../theories/HomogeneousSpaceLaws.v#L122) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.1 | proof route | Federer states "It follows" that A_e = 1_X; the proof chooses g with A(g,x) = x by im A^x = X, so A(e,x) = A(eg,x) = A(g,x) = x. A regression shows that im A^x = X is needed. | Federer gives no argument. | [`homogeneous_unit_identity`](../theories/HomogeneousSpaceLaws.v#L45), [`constant_action_not_unital_example`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L5763) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.1 | proof route | The coset space G/S is specified by its projection (onto, fibres the cosets, quotient topology), with existence from the MathComp generic quotient and quotient topology, sealed; the universal property, openness of the projection and the Hausdorff property and local compactness of G/S are proved. | Federer uses G/S without describing it; these properties are used tacitly. | [`is_left_coset_space`](../theories/CosetSpaceUP.v#L23), [`coset_space_specification`](../theories/CosetSpace.v#L26), [`coset_space_locally_compact`](../theories/CosetSpaceLaws.v#L172) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.1 | hypothesis or convention | The homeomorphism G/G^x ~ X is proved for every space with the specification of G/G^x, with uniqueness of the induced map added. | Strengthening that makes the statement independent of the construction. | [`isotropy_quotient_homeomorphism`](../theories/HomogeneousSpaceCosets.v#L31) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.1 | hypothesis or convention | For a closed subgroup S, the action A(g, hS) = ghS is specified by a predicate with existence and uniqueness; the Hausdorff property of G is not needed, and local compactness of G gives that of G/S. | The printed formula is a definition by cosets. | [`is_coset_action`](../theories/CosetSpaceHomogeneous.v#L24), [`closed_subgroup_coset_homogeneous`](../theories/CosetSpaceHomogeneous.v#L126), [`closed_subgroup_isotropy`](../theories/CosetSpaceHomogeneous.v#L145) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.1 | proof route | Continuity of the character chi of a nonzero covariant integral, which Federer asserts without proof, is proved from a compact neighbourhood bound on the supports of u o A_g, the fixed-support bound of 2.5.13 and local uniform continuity of (x,g) -> u(A(g,x)). | Federer gives no argument. | [`covariant_character_continuous`](../theories/CovariantCharacterContinuity.v#L58), [`covariant_character_homomorphism`](../theories/CovariantCharacterContinuity.v#L116) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.1 | interpretation | The equivalence of chi covariance with A_g#(mu) = chi(g) mu is stated as equality on K(X); properness of A_g, needed for the pushforward of 2.5.19, is proved. | Elements of K(X)* are functionals on K(X). | [`covariant_pushforward`](../theories/CovariantIntegralLaws.v#L72), [`homogeneous_translation_proper`](../theories/CovariantIntegralLaws.v#L42) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.1 | interpretation | "mu+ + mu- is abs(chi) covariant" is the identity on K(X)+, the domain of the variations of 2.5.5; the proof uses the supremum formula of 2.5.6. | The variations are defined on K(X)+ only. | [`covariant_variation_integral`](../theories/CovariantIntegralLaws.v#L172) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.1 | interpretation | "The K(X) regular measure associated with" a monotone covariant integral is the K(X) regular measure representing it, which exists and is unique by 2.5.3. | Federer's phrase presupposes 2.5.2-2.5.3. | [`covariant_measure_exists`](../theories/CovariantIntegralLaws.v#L226), [`is_covariant_measure`](../theories/CovariantIntegralUP.v#L61) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.2 | hypothesis or convention | Both multiplication actions are proved to make G a homogeneous space for every topological group, without local compactness or the Hausdorff property. | Federer calls them special cases of 2.7.1 without proof. | [`left_multiplication_homogeneous`](../theories/GroupMultiplicationHomogeneous.v#L46), [`right_multiplication_homogeneous`](../theories/GroupMultiplicationHomogeneous.v#L93) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.2 | interpretation | A Haar measure is the K(G) regular measure associated with a Haar integral; it is shown to be invariant and not the zero measure, and the converse is not claimed. | Follows the definition of invariant measures in 2.7.1; the converse fails when G is not a countable union of compact sets. | [`is_left_haar_measure`](../theories/HaarIntegralUP.v#L118), [`nonzero_invariant_measure_invariant`](../theories/HaarIntegralLaws.v#L37), [`nonzero_invariant_measure_nonzero`](../theories/HaarIntegralLaws.v#L50) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.2 | proof route | The relation between left and right covariant integrals by p(x) = x^-1 is proved by a general transport lemma for equivariant homeomorphisms; the versions with the actions exchanged and for invariant and Haar integrals are added. | Federer writes "We see" without proof. | [`equivariant_covariant_integral`](../theories/EquivariantPushforward.v#L161), [`left_covariant_inversion`](../theories/HaarInversion.v#L65), [`left_haar_inversion`](../theories/HaarInversion.v#L90) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.3 | interpretation | A uniformity is the predicate [`is_uniformity`](../theories/UniformActionUP.v#L33) on a family of subsets of X x X with [`uniformity_induces_topology`](../theories/UniformActionUP.v#L54), whose fields are those of MathComp's uniform structures; MathComp uniform spaces satisfy it. | "The topology of X is induced by some uniformity" quantifies over uniformities, which MathComp's structure on a type cannot express. | [`is_uniformity`](../theories/UniformActionUP.v#L33), [`acts_uniformly`](../theories/UniformActionUP.v#L75), [`uniform_type_uniformity`](../theories/UniformActionUP.v#L104) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.3 | hypothesis or convention | Necessity of the condition holds for every map A; sufficiency uses only the group laws and the homogeneous space, not local compactness or the Hausdorff property. | Each direction assumes only what its proof uses. | [`acts_uniformly_condition`](../theories/UniformActionLaws.v#L48), [`acts_uniformly_iff`](../theories/UniformActionLaws.v#L305) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.3 | statement correction (local) | The second assertion, that the sets union_g A_g(V) x A_g(V) for nonempty open V form an invariant basis, assumes X nonempty; a regression shows that the family is not a basis for empty X. | For empty X the family is empty, while a uniformity contains X x X. | [`uniform_action_orbit_basis`](../theories/UniformActionLaws.v#L262), [`empty_space_orbit_family_not_basis_example`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L5897) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.3 | proof route | Federer writes "One easily verifies"; the proof of necessity uses sections of a basis entourage, and the proof of sufficiency uses transitivity and the translation law for the sets union_g A_g(V) x A_g(V). | Federer gives no argument. | [`orbit_entourage_translate`](../theories/UniformActionLaws.v#L150), [`orbit_entourage_split`](../theories/UniformActionLaws.v#L220) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.4 | statement correction (local) | The theorem assumes X nonempty; a regression shows that no nonzero invariant integral exists on the empty space although all other hypotheses hold. | For empty X, K(X) = {0}. | [`uniform_action_invariant_integral_exists`](../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L601), [`invariant_integral_empty_space_example`](../tests/MeasureExamples.v#L5983) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.4 | hypothesis or convention | Local compactness and the Hausdorff property of G are not used; (u:w) <= (u:v)(v:w) and the right quotient inequality need only v and w nonzero. | Each result assumes only what its proof uses. | [`haar_ratio_mul`](../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L580), [`haar_ratio_quotient_upper`](../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L628) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.4 | statement correction (erratum) | In the subadditivity estimate, "For any v in S(beta)" and "Since lambda in Closure S(beta)" are replaced by "Whenever 0 <> v in K(X)+, (spt v) x (spt v) subset beta, a in spt v" and "Since lambda in S(beta)" (erratum p. 2, book p. 124 line 30 and p. 125 lines 7-8). | Federer's own correction. | [`haar_limit_superadditive_estimate`](../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L457) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.4 | interpretation | The product P is indexed by all functions X -> R, with the factor {0} outside K(X)+. | The index set of a MathComp product is a type. | [`haar_product_space`](../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L197) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.4 | proof route | lambda is a cluster point of the filter generated by the sets of approximations p_v, because MathComp defines compactness by filters; the printed nested intersection property is proved, and each property of lambda is shown to be closed in the product topology. | Federer argues with closed sets and "like properties of the approximating functions". | [`haar_closed_set_nested`](../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L281), [`haar_limit_exists`](../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L319), [`haar_limit_closed`](../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L346) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.4 | proof route | The sums over G are finitely supported sums handled through finite families (g_i, c_i); r_j = u_j/s with x/0 = 0; the uniform continuity of members of K(X), the limit epsilon -> 0 and the extension of lambda to K(X) by 2.5.13 are made explicit. | Federer calls these steps obvious or leaves them implicit. | [`haar_ratio_add`](../theories/HaarRatioLaws.v#L539), [`compactly_supported_uniformly_continuous`](../theories/UniformityCompactSupport.v#L56), [`haar_limit_superadditive`](../theories/InvariantIntegralExistence.v#L564) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.5 | proof route | The uniformity of both multiplication actions is derived from one statement about a division map; the right case uses the printed sets {(x,y): xy^-1 in V}, not the inversion of 2.7.2. | Federer writes "one sees"; a common proof covers both cases. | [`left_division_entourage_basis`](../theories/GroupUniformity.v#L150), [`right_division_entourage_basis`](../theories/GroupUniformity.v#L177), [`left_multiplication_acts_uniformly`](../theories/GroupUniformity.v#L214) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.5 | hypothesis or convention | The uniformity of the actions uses only the group laws and continuity of the operations; the corollary uses the standing hypotheses on G, and G is nonempty since e in G. | Each result assumes only what its proof uses. | [`left_haar_integral_exists`](../theories/HaarIntegralExistence.v#L33), [`right_haar_integral_exists`](../theories/HaarIntegralExistence.v#L45), [`haar_integrals_exist`](../theories/HaarIntegralExistence.v#L58) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.6 | proof route | "Fubini's theorem" is the order independence of iterated Daniell integrals on K(G x G), from the uniqueness of the 2.6.4 product; no product measure is used, and the membership of the integrands in K(G x G) is proved. | The project's Fubini theorem for Daniell integrals is stated for products of Daniell integrals. | [`haar_fubini_identity`](../theories/HaarIntegralDensity.v#L176), [`compact_daniell_iterations_commute`](../theories/CompactDaniellIterationCommutation.v#L44) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.6 | hypothesis or convention | Positivity of nonzero monotone invariant integrals on nonzero members of K(X)+, used without statement, is proved for every homogeneous space, with the uniqueness of continuous multipliers. | Federer uses it for "positive" and for the uniqueness of k. | [`nonzero_invariant_integral_positive`](../theories/InvariantIntegralPositivity.v#L127), [`nonzero_invariant_integral_multiplier_unique`](../theories/InvariantIntegralPositivity.v#L152) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.6 | proof route | Continuity of k is proved with a cutoff equal to 1 near a point; (1) for every v is derived from the uniqueness of continuous multipliers instead of the independence of the choice of v; independence is proved for every v with mu(v) <> 0. | Federer asserts continuity and infers (1) from the independence for the chosen v. | [`haar_section_integral_continuous`](../theories/HaarIntegralDensity.v#L211), [`haar_density_section_identity`](../theories/HaarIntegralDensity.v#L296), [`haar_integral_density_choice_independent`](../theories/HaarIntegralDensity.v#L279) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.6 | interpretation | "k is uniquely characterized by (2)" is uniqueness among continuous functions; it uses only that mu is a right Haar integral. | mu is defined on K(G) only. | [`haar_integral_density_unique`](../theories/HaarIntegralDensity.v#L265) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.7 | proof route | The right Haar integral of Federer's proof is p#(lambda_1) with p(x) = x^-1, so the theorem does not depend on 2.7.5; the right version is deduced from the left one through p#. | A right Haar integral is obtained from the given left one. | [`left_haar_integral_unique`](../theories/HaarIntegralUniqueness.v#L54), [`right_haar_integral_unique`](../theories/HaarIntegralUniqueness.v#L97) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.7 | hypothesis or convention | The step "hence f = f o A_g^-1" uses that a continuous function is determined by its integrals against K(G), proved from the positivity of Haar integrals. | Federer uses it without statement. | [`left_haar_multiplier_unique`](../theories/InvariantIntegralPositivity.v#L232) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.8 | interpretation | A modular function is any function satisfying [`is_modular_function`](../theories/ModularFunctionUP.v#L66); existence and four uniqueness theorems show that exactly one exists. | Objects are specified by their properties. | [`is_modular_function`](../theories/ModularFunctionUP.v#L66), [`modular_function_exists`](../theories/ModularFunction.v#L225), [`modular_function_unique`](../theories/ModularFunction.v#L325) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.8 | hypothesis or convention | Uniqueness by (3) and (4) assumes the competing function continuous; uniqueness by (1) and (2) assumes nothing, not even positivity. | lambda and mu are defined on K(G) only, and continuity puts the integrands in K(G). | [`modular_function_unique_left_inversion`](../theories/ModularFunction.v#L276), [`modular_function_unique_left_haar`](../theories/ModularFunction.v#L238) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.8 | hypothesis or convention | The homomorphism property of Delta_G, used implicitly in 2.7.9(3), is proved as a corollary. | Federer does not state it. | [`modular_function_homomorphism`](../theories/ModularFunction.v#L337) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.9 | interpretation | The character chi is given by three hypotheses (multiplicative, nonvanishing, continuous); chi(e) = 1 and chi(g^-1) = chi(g)^-1 are derived. | A continuous homomorphism into R - {0} is expressed by its laws. | [`left_covariant_haar_characterisation`](../theories/CovariantHaarCharacterisation.v#L322), [`character_unit`](../theories/CovariantHaarCharacterisation.v#L211) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.9 | proof route | The invariance of sigma+ and sigma-, asserted by Federer, combines the abs(chi) covariance of 2.7.1 with sigma = sigma+ - sigma-; the variations are extended from K(G)+ to monotone Daniell integrals on K(G), each zero or a left Haar integral, before 2.7.7 is applied; sigma is a Daniell integral by 2.5.13. | Federer leaves these steps implicit. | [`invariant_integral_variations_invariant`](../theories/InvariantDaniellVariation.v#L123), [`invariant_integral_variation_extensions`](../theories/InvariantDaniellVariation.v#L148), [`left_invariant_variations_haar_multiples`](../theories/CovariantHaarCharacterisation.v#L118) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.9 | proof route | In (3), 2.7.8(1) is applied to the two monotone parts of sigma; the converse "by use of p#" is carried out on p#(nu) with the character Delta_G chi^-1, whose multiplicativity comes from 2.7.1 when nu is nonzero. | Federer applies 2.7.8(1) to sigma = r lambda and uses the multiplicativity of Delta_G without comment. | [`left_covariant_right_modular`](../theories/CovariantHaarCharacterisation.v#L339), [`left_covariant_modular_characterisation`](../theories/CovariantHaarCharacterisation.v#L436) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.10 | interpretation | The isotropy group G^x is the subtype with the relative topology and restricted operations, proved to be a locally compact Hausdorff topological group; mu^x is specified by its laws on K(G), with uniqueness proved. | Federer regards G^x as a group and mu^x as determined without comment. | [`is_subgroup_restriction`](../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L104), [`subgroup_restriction_locally_compact_hausdorff`](../theories/IsotropyGroupTopology.v#L211), [`is_coset_integral`](../theories/CosetIntegralUP.v#L29), [`coset_integral_unique`](../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L215) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.10 | proof route | mu^x(u) is obtained from the openness and surjectivity of A^x, not the homeomorphism G/G^x ~ X; the function f of (5) is a cutoff equal to 1 on finitely many compact neighbourhoods; the continuity of w adds the fixed-support bound of 2.6.4. | Federer uses a base of X and leaves the estimate implicit. | [`open_map_factor_continuous`](../theories/CosetIntegralExistence.v#L48), [`coset_integral_cover`](../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L374), [`coset_section_integral_continuous`](../theories/CosetIntegralExistence.v#L100) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.10 | hypothesis or convention | (2) and (5) use the positivity of Haar integrals of 2.7.6; (3) is proved for every continuous v; only the Hausdorff property of X is used; v/mu^x(f) is the pointwise quotient with r/0 = 0. | Federer writes "easily verified"; the generalisations cost nothing. | [`coset_integral_support`](../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L310), [`coset_integral_multiplier`](../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L328), [`coset_integral_quotient`](../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L421) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.10 | proof route | (6) uses, besides (1), (4), (5), the monotonicity and monotone convergence of mu^x. | Federer writes "(6) follows from (1), (4), (5)". | [`coset_integral_daniell`](../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L476), [`coset_integral_covariant`](../theories/CosetIntegralLaws.v#L499) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.11 | interpretation | Delta_{G^x} is a modular function of the subtype group G^x; Weil's condition is the predicate [`weil_condition`](../theories/WeilCondition.v#L59); (2) is nu_2 = c nu_1 on K(X) with c <> 0. | The isotropy group is formalised as in 2.7.10. | [`weil_condition`](../theories/WeilCondition.v#L59), [`covariant_integral_exists_iff_weil`](../theories/WeilCondition.v#L208), [`covariant_integral_unique_multiple`](../theories/WeilCondition.v#L241) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.11 | proof route | The sufficiency proof chooses lambda(u) = lambda_0(u chi^-1) for a left Haar integral lambda_0 (2.7.5, 2.7.9); nu is defined by choosing preimages under mu^x and shown to be a Daniell integral by the order-boundedness criterion of 2.5.13; the necessity step uses 2.7.8(1) for mu. | Federer writes "use 2.7.9 to choose" and "we see from 2.7.10(5), (6)". | [`coset_integral_exchange`](../theories/CovariantIntegralFactorisation.v#L240), [`coset_integral_factor_exists`](../theories/CovariantIntegralFactorisation.v#L322), [`coset_integral_factor_daniell`](../theories/CovariantIntegralFactorisation.v#L352) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.11 | hypothesis or convention | The exchange and factorisation lemmas hold for every continuous psi equal to Delta_{G^x} on G^x; the nonnegativity of Federer's w is not used. | The printed case psi = Delta_G chi^-1 is an instance. | [`coset_integral_factorisation`](../theories/CovariantIntegralFactorisation.v#L422) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.12 | proof route | The facts that continuous homomorphisms of compact groups into the positive reals are 1, that A^x is proper for compact G^x, and the equivalence of unimodularity with Delta_G = 1 are proved; Federer gives no argument. | Federer states them without proof. | [`compact_group_positive_character_unit`](../theories/CompactGroupCharacters.v#L95), [`compact_isotropy_orbit_proper`](../theories/CompactIsotropyIntegrals.v#L125), [`unimodular_iff_modular_unit`](../theories/UnimodularGroups.v#L40) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.12 | hypothesis or convention | The identity with nu is proved as [A^x#(lambda) o mu^x] u = mu(1) lambda(u) for every left Haar integral mu of G^x; the existence of a normalised mu with mu(1) = 1, and the identity for it, are proved separately, with the local compactness of X as an explicit hypothesis; pushforward covariance holds for every chi. | The scaled identity covers the normalised case. | [`compact_isotropy_normalised_haar_exists`](../theories/CompactIsotropyNormalisation.v#L68), [`compact_isotropy_normalised_factor`](../theories/CompactIsotropyNormalisation.v#L113), [`compact_isotropy_pushforward_coset_scaled`](../theories/CompactIsotropyIntegrals.v#L284), [`compact_isotropy_pushforward_factor`](../theories/CompactIsotropyIntegrals.v#L312), [`compact_isotropy_pushforward_covariant`](../theories/CompactIsotropyIntegrals.v#L174) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.12 | interpretation | "The left and right Haar integrals coincide" is equality of the two classes of functionals; normality is the predicate [`is_normal_subgroup`](../theories/NormalSubgroupUP.v#L16); G/S is any model of the coset space, with the structure of S transported to the isotropy group of pi(e). | Federer does not define these notions. | [`is_unimodular`](../theories/UnimodularGroupUP.v#L21), [`is_normal_subgroup`](../theories/NormalSubgroupUP.v#L16), [`coset_space_invariant_integral_iff`](../theories/CosetSpaceInvariantIntegrals.v#L61) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.12 | proof route | For normal S the invariant integral on G/S comes from Federer's alternative "because G acts uniformly on G/S" (2.7.3-2.7.4); his first reason, through the group G/S, is not formalised. | It avoids building the topological group structure on G/S. | [`normal_coset_action_acts_uniformly`](../theories/CosetSpaceInvariantIntegrals.v#L172), [`normal_subgroup_modular_restriction`](../theories/CosetSpaceInvariantIntegrals.v#L216), [`modular_kernel_unimodular`](../theories/CosetSpaceInvariantIntegrals.v#L278) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.13 | interpretation | L_1 is the total-domain space of the erratum with members up to null functions; "linear epimorphism" is additivity and homogeneity almost everywhere and surjectivity up to null functions; the fibre formula holds at gamma almost all points, for every representative g. | A null modification of beta^x preserves the laws, so the fibre formula cannot hold everywhere. | [`is_coset_lone_integral`](../theories/CosetLoneIntegralUP.v#L53), [`coset_lone_integral_epimorphism`](../theories/CosetLoneIntegralEpimorphism.v#L74), [`coset_lone_integral_unique`](../theories/CosetLoneIntegralLaws.v#L134) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.13 | proof route | T(u) is any gamma summable function satisfying the fibre formula almost everywhere, avoiding a choice of representatives of the points gx; Federer's method of 2.5.3 is followed, with the F_2 case and the passage to all summable functions made explicit. | Federer's definition presupposes independence of the representative. | [`coset_lone_class`](../theories/CosetLoneClass.v#L45), [`coset_lone_integral_exists`](../theories/CosetLoneIntegralExistence.v#L328) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.13 | proof route | Surjectivity lifts members of K(X)+ by 2.7.10(5), uses the density of K(X)+ in L_1(gamma) and sums the lifted increments by increasing convergence, instead of treating subsets of a compact set through F_j[K(X)]. | A direct density argument. | [`coset_lone_integral_onto`](../theories/CosetLoneIntegralOnto.v#L374) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.13 | hypothesis or convention | That nu is a monotone Daniell integral follows from its representation; the regularity of beta is not used; the Haar property of mu is used only for surjectivity; only the Hausdorff property of X is used. | Each step assumes only what it uses. | [`coset_lone_integral_epimorphism`](../theories/CosetLoneIntegralEpimorphism.v#L74) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.14 | statement correction (erratum) | The theorem is proved when the integral does not vanish on K(G) (erratum p. 3, book p. 131 line 10: "nonzero monotone left (right) chi covariant integral") or G is a countable union of compact sets; for the zero integral on a group that is not, the associated measure is infinite on G and zero on compact sets, hence not Radon (formalised counterexample on the subsets of N under symmetric difference). | The printed statement is false for the zero integral; Federer's correction adds "nonzero". | [`left_covariant_integral_radon`](../theories/CovariantMeasureRadon.v#L107), [`zero_left_invariant_measure_not_radon`](../theories/CovariantMeasureRadonCounterexample.v#L128) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.14 | statement correction (erratum) | The misprints P = intersection over Phi (read with Psi; erratum p. 3, book p. 132 lines 22 and 25) and "U = (X - P) cup (P cap U)" (read with G for X; erratum p. 3, book p. 133 line 3) are corrected. | Read literally, P = G - U and the argument is circular. | [`covariant_open_compact_closure_measurable`](../theories/CovariantMeasureOpenMeasurability.v#L784) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.14 | proof route | The quotient G/S and its metric are not formed: finitely many pseudometrics rho_f on G, total boundedness of Clos U and Chebyshev estimates replace the countable base of A^x(U) and the mu^x/beta/Fubini computation; Psi is modified to contain a nonzero member; the right version is deduced from the opposite group. | A direct argument on G avoids building the metric quotient. | [`CovariantMeasureOpenMeasurability.v`](../theories/CovariantMeasureOpenMeasurability.v), [`right_covariant_measure_radon`](../theories/CovariantMeasureRadon.v#L224) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.14 | hypothesis or convention | Inner regularity of open sets of infinite measure, which the reduction "in view of 2.5.3, 2.5.14" does not give, is proved from the added hypothesis through an open sigma compact subgroup and its cosets. | Federer's reduction leaves this case open. | [`RegularMeasureRadonCriteria.v`](../theories/RegularMeasureRadonCriteria.v), [`OpenSigmaCompactSubgroup.v`](../theories/OpenSigmaCompactSubgroup.v) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.15 | statement correction (erratum) | As in 2.7.14, the corollary is proved when the integral does not vanish on K(X) (erratum p. 3, book p. 133 line 4: "nonzero monotone chi covariant integral") or X is a countable union of compact sets; the counterexample of 2.7.14 (X = G) shows the hypothesis is needed. | The printed statement is false for the zero integral; Federer's correction adds "nonzero". | [`homogeneous_covariant_measure_radon`](../theories/HomogeneousCovariantMeasureRadon.v#L393) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.15 | proof route | Only the measurability part of 2.7.14 is used for alpha; U is built from 2.7.10(5) with a cutoff; V = {beta^x(u) > 0} holds gamma almost everywhere, which suffices for measurability. | beta^x(u) is determined only up to null sets. | [`homogeneous_open_compact_closure_measurable`](../theories/HomogeneousCovariantMeasureRadon.v#L338) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(1) | statement correction (erratum) | "L is the unique" is read as "L^n is the unique" (erratum p. 3, book p. 134 line 26), and the exponent of the recursion for alpha(n) is (n-1)/2, not (n-2)/2 (erratum p. 3, book p. 135 line 35). | Federer's own corrections. | [`euclidean_lebesgue_haar_unique`](../theories/EuclideanLebesgueHaar.v#L224), [`euclidean_ball_recursion`](../theories/EuclideanBallVolume.v#L224) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(1) | proof route | The K(R^n) regularity of L^n and the Haar property of f#(L^n) are proved through a general principle for translation invariant Borel regular measures on commutative groups; uniqueness uses 2.7.7 and a comparison of Borel regular measures representing proportional functionals. | Federer uses these facts tacitly. | [`invariant_borel_function_lattice_regular`](../theories/TranslationInvariantHaar.v#L171), [`represented_borel_regular_scaled`](../theories/RepresentingMeasureComparison.v#L177), [`euclidean_lebesgue_left_haar`](../theories/EuclideanLebesgueHaar.v#L173) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(1) | proof route | The determinant formula composes the image measures of the factorisation f o g = h o d of 1.7.3 (g, h orthogonal, d diagonal) instead of proving that the subgroup H is GL(n,R); det f = 0 is treated through the adjoint and a null hyperplane; SL transitivity is proved by an explicit construction; the simplex integral uses the Riemann criterion, since 2.9.20 is not formalised. | Direct arguments where Federer cites unformalised results or gives none. | [`euclidean_lebesgue_linear_image`](../theories/EuclideanLebesgueDeterminant.v#L198), [`special_linear_transitive`](../theories/SpecialLinearTransitive.v#L156), [`euclidean_standard_simplex_measure`](../theories/EuclideanSimplexVolume.v#L222) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(1) | interpretation | GL(n,R) and SL(n,R) are types of linear maps acting by evaluation, with covariance stated on R^n; the non-existence of GL invariant Radon measures reads invariance as invariance of the measure of every set and is proved directly by a box argument. | Federer's argument applies 2.7.11(2) to R^n ~ {0} as a homogeneous space of SL(n,R), whose topology part (1) does not use. | [`euclidean_lebesgue_general_linear_covariant`](../theories/EuclideanLinearGroupCovariance.v#L79), [`no_general_linear_invariant_radon`](../theories/LinearInvariantRadon.v#L184) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16 | interpretation | Hom(V, W) carries the topology of the inner product of 1.7.9, with the same neighbourhoods as the operator norm of 1.7.6 and of every other norm (see the rows on norms on A(V, V)). The linear group is the type of univalent members of Hom(V, V) with the relative topology and sealed operations; the affine group is the product GL(V) x V transported by M, homeomorphic to the affine group with the relative topology of A(V, V). | MathComp has no norm-independent topology on finite-dimensional spaces; Federer uses one norm for the estimates. | [`hom_space_nbhs_operator`](../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v#L167), [`hom_additive_lch_group`](../theories/HomSpaceTopology.v#L408), [`linear_group_operations_lch`](../theories/LinearGroupSealed.v#L117), [`affine_group_map_onto`](../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L204), [`affine_group_lch`](../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L344) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16 | proof route | Proofs Federer omits are supplied: local compactness of GL(V) from its openness in Hom(V, V); the openness of the orbit maps of GL(n, R) and SL(n, R) on R^n ~ {0} by explicit perturbations; the closedness of SL(n, R) from the continuity of det; the compactness of O(n) as a closed bounded set. Isometries are characterised by expanding the single quantity \|g(tx + y) - t g(x) - g(y)\|^2 = 0, without assuming surjectivity, for any finite-dimensional inner product space. | Federer states these facts without proof or with two separate expansions. | [`linear_group_lch_group`](../theories/LinearGroupTopology.v#L406), [`punctured_linear_homogeneous`](../theories/GeneralLinearPunctured.v#L266), [`special_linear_punctured_homogeneous`](../theories/SpecialLinearTopology.v#L412), [`special_linear_closed`](../theories/SpecialLinearTopology.v#L110), [`orthogonal_maps_compact`](../theories/OrthogonalGroupTopology.v#L165), [`isometry_group_maps`](../theories/AffineGroupTopology.v#L429) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16 | interpretation | "Which really does not matter because L^n{0} = 0" is formalised as: the restriction of L^n to subsets of R^n ~ {0} is a \|det\|^{-1} covariant measure for GL(n, R) and an invariant measure for SL(n, R), for n >= 1; the topological GL(n, R) is a new type, and the covariance of part (1) is restated for it. | Part (1) states the covariance on R^n for a type without topology. | [`punctured_lebesgue_covariant`](../theories/PuncturedLebesgueCovariance.v#L430), [`punctured_lebesgue_special_linear_invariant`](../theories/PuncturedLebesgueCovariance.v#L446), [`euclidean_lebesgue_linear_group_covariant`](../theories/GeneralLinearPunctured.v#L120) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16 | proof route | The equivalence of all norms on a finite-dimensional space is proved through the positive minimum of a norm on the compact unit sphere of an inner product; M is a homeomorphism by comparison with the norm \|L(f)\| + \|f(0)\|; continuity of composition on A(V, V) is reduced to Hom(V, V) by M(f o g) = (L(f) o L(g), f(0) + L(f)(g(0))); openness and density of the affine group follow from those of the linear group through L and M; continuity of inversion is transported from the representation by pairs. | Federer states these facts and writes "using the maps L, M, tau we infer". | [`finite_norms_equivalent`](../theories/FiniteNormEquivalence.v#L95), [`affine_space_iso_homeomorphism`](../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L318), [`affine_composition_continuous`](../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L381), [`affine_space_group_open`](../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L434), [`affine_space_group_dense`](../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L457), [`affine_inversion_continuous`](../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L591) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16 | interpretation | A(V, V) is specified by [`is_affine_map_space`](../theories/AffineMapSpaceUP.v#L40) (a space with an evaluation onto the affine maps, pointwise operations), with a sealed subtype for existence; "induce the same topology" is the equality of neighbourhood filters; composition and inversion are specified by their values; the results are stated for A(V, W) and Hom(V, W), with dim A(V, W) = nk + k. | Federer's vectorspace of affine maps and his topological phrasing; generalisation. | [`affine_maps_finite_dimensional`](../theories/AffineMapSpaceSealed.v#L71), [`finite_norms_same_nbhs`](../theories/FiniteNormEquivalence.v#L110), [`hom_space_norm_same_nbhs`](../theories/HomSpaceNormTopology.v#L50), [`affine_space_group_lch`](../theories/AffineMapSpaceTopology.v#L631) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(2) | statement correction (erratum) | The left Haar integral of GL(V) is lambda(u) = int u \|det\|^{-n} dphi; the book prints \|det\|^n (erratum p. 3, book p. 136 line 29). | Federer's own correction. | [`linear_group_left_haar_integral`](../theories/LinearGroupUnimodular.v#L324) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(2) | proof route | The covariance factor of f \|-> g o f is computed as \|det(f \|-> g o f)\|^{-1} = \|det g\|^{-m} by transport of phi to Lebesgue measure on R^N, not through a product of Haar measures on V^m; the null complement of the monomorphisms uses phi = c L^N o Phi^{-1} and 2.6.5; the right \|det\|^n covariance computes the determinant of f \|-> f o g^{-1} through wedge^1 g on Hom(V, R) instead of pushing phi forward by wedge^1. | Direct determinant computations; no vectors and covectors are identified. | [`hom_haar_integral_covariant`](../theories/HomHaarCovariance.v#L296), [`finite_haar_integral_linear`](../theories/FiniteHaarLinear.v#L381), [`hom_monomorphisms_complement_null`](../theories/HomMonomorphisms.v#L351), [`linear_group_haar_right_covariant`](../theories/LinearGroupUnimodular.v#L285), [`dual_map_det`](../theories/BlockDeterminant.v#L223) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(2) | interpretation | The covariance on X = Hom(U, V), which is not a homogeneous space, is the identity of 2.7.1 for the action g o f, without m <= n; "phi over G" and "phi over the monomorphisms" are the restrictions of phi, identified with the K regular measures associated with the integrals u \|-> int u' dphi of the extension by zero; the monomorphisms are shown to be a homogeneous space with an associated covariant measure; U and V carry inner products for the topology. | Federer's phrasing presupposes these readings. | [`hom_haar_measure_covariant`](../theories/HomHaarCovariance.v#L317), [`hom_monomorphisms_homogeneous`](../theories/HomMonomorphismSpace.v#L292), [`monomorphism_covariant_measure`](../theories/HomMonomorphismSpace.v#L355), [`linear_group_unimodular`](../theories/LinearGroupUnimodular.v#L367) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(3) | interpretation | X is defined as {f : f[W] ⊂ W} and proved to be the subspace generated by G; "map W onto W" is W = g[W]; the covariance on X, which is not a homogeneous space, is stated for the action g o f; "over G" means the restriction to the open set of units, identified with the K(G) regular measure of the integral there; the null complement is stated for every Haar measure of X instead of phi x psi. | Federer's phrasing presupposes these readings, as in example (2). | [`subspace_preserving_maps_generated`](../theories/StabiliserHomSpace.v#L690), [`maps_subspace_onto`](../theories/SubspaceStabiliser.v#L273), [`preserving_haar_measure_covariant`](../theories/StabiliserHaarCovariance.v#L244), [`preserving_units_complement_null`](../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L305) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(3) | proof route | The factor chi(g) is computed as \|det(f \|-> g o f)\|^{-1} in the base of X corresponding to Hom(W, W) x Hom(Z, V), without the product phi x psi; the transport by wedge^1 to X' and G' is replaced by the determinant of f \|-> f o g^{-1}, with chi' written out at wedge^1 g^{-1}; det(wedge^1 g) = det(g\|W) det(wedge^1 g\|W') is proved by a block triangular determinant; the non-unimodularity is shown by the element 1 on W, 2 on Z. | Direct determinant computations; Federer gives no proof of the determinant identity and no element. | [`preserving_haar_integral_covariant`](../theories/StabiliserHaarCovariance.v#L220), [`preserving_right_composition_det`](../theories/StabiliserHomSpace.v#L487), [`restriction_annihilator_det`](../theories/StabiliserHomSpace.v#L584), [`triangular_block_det`](../theories/BlockTriangularDeterminant.v#L207), [`subspace_stabiliser_not_unimodular`](../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L666) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(3) | hypothesis or convention | Delta_G(g) = \|det(g\|W)\|^{-n} \|det g\|^m is proved without 0 < m < n; V carries an inner product and a finite base for the topology of X. | Generalisation and added structure, as in example (2). | [`subspace_stabiliser_modular`](../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L645), [`euclidean_subspace_stabiliser_modular`](../theories/StabiliserModularFunction.v#L698) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(2) | proof route | The restriction of a K(T) regular measure on a locally compact metric space with a countable base to an open subset is proved to be the K(S) regular measure associated with the restricted integral; this gives the covariant measures over G in (2) and (3) and over the monomorphisms. Federer's steps through wedge^1 (wedge^1 maps G onto G', (wedge^1)_# phi is a Haar measure, the diagram) are proved, but the right \|det\|^n covariance is still derived from the direct determinant computation. | Federer passes from phi to phi over G without comment. | [`open_subspace_measure_associated`](../theories/OpenSubspaceMeasure.v#L298), [`linear_group_haar_measure_left_covariant`](../theories/LinearGroupHaarMeasure.v#L103), [`linear_group_haar_measure_right_covariant`](../theories/LinearGroupHaarMeasure.v#L155), [`exterior_one_haar_measure`](../theories/DualMapHaarMeasure.v#L341), [`stabiliser_haar_measure_right_modular`](../theories/StabiliserHaarMeasure.v#L182) (2.7.md) |

| 2.7.16(4) | proof route | Supplied proofs: {g : det g = 1} is the commutator subgroup (transvections are commutators; reduction column by column); G(n, m) and the nonzero simple m-vectors are homogeneous spaces of GL(n, R) (continuity through a bound for differences of orthogonal projections and the norm of 1.7.5, openness from O(n)); the isotropy group at xi and its modular function \|det\|^m through 2.7.12. | Federer presupposes the homogeneous spaces and asserts the commutator subgroup without proof. | [`special_linear_commutator`](../theories/SpecialLinearCommutators.v#L465), [`linear_grassmann_homogeneous`](../theories/LinearGrassmannAction.v#L317), [`simple_vector_homogeneous`](../theories/SimpleVectorHomogeneous.v#L466), [`simple_isotropy_modular`](../theories/SimpleVectorWeilCondition.v#L262), [`simple_vector_covariant_integral`](../theories/SimpleVectorWeilCondition.v#L324) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(4) | hypothesis or convention | The nonexistence is stated for every function chi (a nonzero chi covariant integral forces chi to be a continuous homomorphism); Weil's condition is an equivalence for continuous homomorphisms; the integral on simple m-vectors is proved for 0 < m <= n; V is any finite-dimensional inner product space. | Generalisation. | [`grassmann_weil_condition`](../theories/GrassmannWeilCondition.v#L81), [`euclidean_grassmann_no_covariant_integral`](../theories/GrassmannWeilCondition.v#L167), [`euclidean_simple_vector_covariant_integral`](../theories/SimpleVectorWeilCondition.v#L370) (2.7.md) |

| 2.7.16(5) | proof route | Supplied steps: every Haar integral of V is \|det o L\|^{-1} covariant; G^0 is isomorphic to GL(n, R) and modular functions are transported; Delta_G = 1 on ker L since ker L is a closed normal abelian subgroup (2.7.12); X is a homogeneous space of the affine group; "argue as above" is carried out with W in place of R^n, and the nonexistence is stated for every chi. The affine group is the group of pairs (g, z). | Federer gives these steps without proof. | [`affine_group_modular`](../theories/AffineGroupModular.v#L297), [`affine_haar_integral_covariant`](../theories/AffineGroupModular.v#L123), [`abelian_normal_modular_unit`](../theories/GroupIsomorphismModular.v#L160), [`affine_subspace_affine_homogeneous`](../theories/AffineSubspaceAffineAction.v#L156), [`affine_subspace_no_covariant_integral`](../theories/AffineSubspaceWeilCondition.v#L117) (2.7.md) |
