The proofs normalise a Haar integral by its value at 1, use 2.7.7 and 2.5.3 for uniqueness, 2.5.19 and 2.7.12 for invariance, the coincidence of Radon measures representing the same integral, and derive the openness of the orbit maps from compactness. | Supplied proofs. | [`compact_group_unique_haar_measure`](../theories/CompactGroupHaarNormalisation.v#L192), [`compact_orbit_pushforward_invariant`](../theories/CompactGroupHomogeneousMeasures.v#L280), [`radon_representations_coincide`](../theories/CompactGroupHomogeneousMeasures.v#L87), [`compact_transitive_homogeneous`](../theories/CompactGroupHomogeneousMeasures.v#L174) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(6) | hypothesis or convention | Both general assertions are proved for every compact group with the standing hypothesis of 2.7.1 and for O(V) with V any finite-dimensional inner product space; of the hypotheses on X only the Hausdorff property is assumed; existence statements take a point of X; theta_{n,m}, theta*_{n,m}, gamma_{n,m} are obtained for m <= n. | Generalisation; an empty space carries no measure of total measure 1, and O(n, m) is empty for n < m. | [`normalised_invariant_measure_exists`](../theories/CompactGroupHomogeneousMeasures.v#L365), [`orthogonal_group_unique_haar_measure`](../theories/OrthogonalGroupHaarMeasure.v#L137), [`euclidean_theta_nm_exists_unique`](../theories/EuclideanOrthogonalInvariantMeasures.v#L141) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(6) | interpretation | "Unique left and right Haar measure" means that every left or right Haar measure of total measure 1 equals theta_n; "the" invariant measures are shown unique and independent of x. O(n, m) and O*(n, m) carry relative topologies, G(n, m) the initial topology of W \|-> P_W; O(n) acts by g o f, p o g^{-1} and g[W]; O*(n, m) is the set of orthogonal projections, equal to {f* : f in O(n, m)}. | Federer names neither the topologies nor the actions; 3.2.28(4) is not used. | [`compact_invariant_measure_unique`](../theories/CompactGroupHomogeneousMeasures.v#L312), [`orthogonal_projections_homogeneous`](../theories/OrthogonalInjectionSpaces.v#L452), [`grassmann_space_homogeneous`](../theories/GrassmannHomogeneousSpace.v#L454), [`euclidean_theta_star_nm_exists_unique`](../theories/EuclideanOrthogonalInvariantMeasures.v#L159) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(6) | interpretation | beta_t(n, m) is specified by the predicate [`is_projection_constant`](../theories/OrthogonalProjectionConstants.v#L286) over every normalised invariant measure on O*(n, m) and every restricted structure of O(n), with existence and uniqueness proved; beta_oo(n, m) = 1 is the value at +oo of [`is_extended_projection_constant`](../theories/ProjectionConstantNorms.v#L54); [theta*_{n,m}]_(t)(f) = beta_t(n, m) \|xi\| is stated with the specification of the L_t norm of 2.4.12. | No function beta_t is defined; the public L_t value would need a separate measurability hypothesis. | [`projection_constant_exists`](../theories/OrthogonalProjectionConstants.v#L322), [`projection_constant_unique`](../theories/OrthogonalProjectionConstants.v#L390), [`projection_norm_seminorm`](../theories/ProjectionConstantNorms.v#L187) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(6) | hypothesis or convention | The constants, the integral over O(n) and the corollary are proved for every t > 0 and also for m = 0 and n = 0, where Federer has 1 <= t and positive n >= m; the display \|zeta ^ eta\| = \|<zeta, wedge_m q>\| is proved for simple zeta, where Federer writes "for any zeta", and is applied, as by Federer, to zeta = <xi, wedge_m g>. | Generalisation, and a restriction of an auxiliary display to the case used. | [`orthogonal_wedge_integral`](../theories/OrthogonalWedgeIntegrals.v#L207), [`wedge_kernel_projection_exists`](../theories/WedgeKernelProjection.v#L93), [`projection_constant_ratio`](../theories/OrthogonalWedgeIntegrals.v#L419) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(6) | proof route | Existence of beta_t uses the transitivity of O(n) on orthonormal m-frames (Federer's two quoted facts are proved separately); Fubini's theorem is applied in its Daniell form on K(O(n) x O*(n-k, m)) instead of 2.6.2; the existence of q, the inner integral over O(n) (via theta*_{n,m} = (A^p)_# theta_n and inversion invariance) and the case t = oo of the L_t norm are supplied. | Federer's argument is a sketch; the integrand is continuous on a compact space. | [`simple_unit_transitive`](../theories/SimpleUnitTransitivity.v#L71), [`projection_integral_rotation`](../theories/SimpleUnitTransitivity.v#L143), [`compact_haar_integral_inversion`](../theories/OrthogonalWedgeIntegrals.v#L67), [`projection_norm_essential`](../theories/ProjectionConstantNorms.v#L100) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(7) | statement correction (local) | In "s[p^{-1}{y}] = (p s^{-1})^{-1}{y} for s in O(n), y in R^n" the variable y ranges over R^{n-m}. | p maps R^n into R^{n-m}. | [`affine_parametrisation_rotate`](../theories/AffineSubspaceParametrisation.v#L159), [`affine_invariant_integral_alternate`](../theories/AffineSubspaceInvariantMeasure.v#L413) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(7) | interpretation | The space X of m dimensional affine subspaces, which Federer gives no topology, carries the initial topology of S \|-> (dir S, nearest S) into G(n, m) x R^n (equivalently the nearest point retraction in A(R^n, R^n)); delta(S) is \|nearest S\|; theta_n^0 is mu^0 of 2.7.10 for any Haar integral represented by theta_n, the value of lambda not depending on the choice; the alternate formula holds for every normalised invariant measure on O*(n, n - m). | Federer presupposes the topology and the identifications. | [`affine_subspace_space`](../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L338), [`affine_subspace_homogeneous`](../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L715), [`isometry_haar_integral_theta`](../theories/IsometryGroupHaar.v#L492) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(7) | proof route | Supplied proofs: L^n is G invariant (orthogonal maps and translations); unimodularity by Weil's condition on G^0 and Delta_G(tau_z)^2 = 1 from sigma o tau_z = tau_{-z} o sigma, sigma = -1; ker p is m dimensional; continuity, openness and properness of xi; invariance of mu by Fubini's theorem in Daniell form; theta_n x L^{n-m} is a Radon measure, proved by comparing Borel regular measures with a Radon measure on open sets, without a metric on the product. | Federer states these facts with "we see that" or presupposes them. | [`isometry_group_unimodular`](../theories/IsometryGroupHaar.v#L424), [`projection_kernel_exists`](../theories/OrthogonalProjectionFibres.v#L100), [`affine_parametrisation_proper`](../theories/AffineSubspaceParametrisation.v#L337), [`compact_product_measure_radon`](../theories/CompactProductRadon.v#L246), [`radon_agreement`](../theories/CompactProductRadon.v#L187), [`affine_invariant_measure_invariant`](../theories/AffineSubspaceInvariantMeasure.v#L385) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.16(7) | hypothesis or convention | m <= n is assumed and p in O*(n, n - m) is a parameter; the topological results on X and on G hold for every finite-dimensional inner product space V. | Generalisation. | [`affine_subspace_space_locally_compact`](../theories/AffineSubspaceSpace.v#L731), [`isometry_product_homeomorphism`](../theories/IsometryGroupStructure.v#L150) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.17 | interpretation | "G is a compact topological group" is read within the standing hypothesis of 2.7.1 (locally compact Hausdorff group) together with compactness of G; "countably infinite" is countable and infinite; "any Haar measure" covers left and right Haar measures of 2.7.2. | Federer's standing hypotheses and definitions. | [`compact_group_transversal_nonmeasurable`](../theories/CompactGroupNonmeasurable.v#L215) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.17 | proof route | The steps of the proof are supplied: left and right Haar measures of a compact group coincide (from 2.7.12); phi(T s) = phi(T) and the measurability of T s follow from phi(A_g^{-1}[B]) = phi(B), proved by the uniqueness of 2.5.3 and the regularity of image measures under homeomorphisms; phi(T) = 0 contradicts phi(G) > 0, and 0 < phi(G) < oo is derived from 2.5.14. | Federer gives no argument for these steps. | [`compact_group_haar_measures_coincide`](../theories/CompactGroupNonmeasurable.v#L131), [`invariant_measure_preimage`](../theories/InvariantMeasureTranslates.v#L124), [`lattice_regular_inverse_pushforward`](../theories/InvariantMeasureTranslates.v#L94), [`compact_right_haar_measure_total`](../theories/CompactGroupNonmeasurable.v#L145) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.17 | hypothesis or convention | The existence of a transversal T is proved by the axiom of choice; for Vitali's example R/Z is specified by its projection and the laws of its operations, with existence, compactness and the countable infinite subgroup Q/Z supplied; Vitali's set in R is not treated. | Federer presupposes T and gives no details of the example. | [`left_coset_transversal_exists`](../theories/CompactGroupNonmeasurable.v#L87), [`is_real_integer_quotient_group`](../theories/VitaliNonmeasurableSet.v#L109), [`vitali_nonmeasurable_set_exists`](../theories/VitaliNonmeasurableSet.v#L345) (2.7.md) |

| 2.7.18 | proof route | The identity int \|v - w\|_g dgamma = chi(g) int \|v - w\| dgamma is proved through A_g#(gamma) = chi(g) gamma (2.5.19, 2.4.18 and the uniqueness of 2.5.3), with chi(g) > 0 and integration with respect to chi(g) gamma supplied; if the associated integral is 0, chi may be discontinuous and the argument is applied to the character 1. | Federer uses the identity without proof and the continuity of chi, which holds only for a nonzero integral. | [`covariant_translation_image`](../theories/CovariantMeasureImage.v#L103), `covariant_character_positive` ([`CovariantMeasureImage.v`](../theories/CovariantMeasureImage.v)), [`covariant_measure_positive_character`](../theories/CovariantTranslationContinuity.v#L117), [`measure_scale_integral`](../theories/MeasureScaleIntegral.v#L216) (2.7.md) |
| 2.7.18 | proof route | The density of K(X) in L_1(gamma) is applied to max(v,0) and max(-v,0); the pointwise estimate is replaced by the triangle inequality of the L_1 seminorm; uniform continuity of w is replaced by local uniform continuity of (x,g) \|-> w(A(g,x)) on a compact set and the fixed-support bound of 2.5.13. | No uniform structure on X is used. | [`regular_vector_lattice_lone_dense`](../theories/CovariantTranslationContinuity.v#L61), [`compact_translation_lone_small`](../theories/CovariantTranslationContinuity.v#L147) (2.7.md) |

## 2.8 Covering theorems

| Item | Kind | Divergence | Reason | Rocq |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2.8.1 | hypothesis or convention | The ball formulas are defined for all real radii, including zero; fineness still quantifies only over positive radii, as printed. Boundedness is expressed by finite extended diameter, with empty diameter zero. | Reuses the existing metric and sealed diameter interfaces and makes the strict/open versus closed boundary explicit. | [`metric_open_ball`](../theories/CoveringFamilyUP.v#L22), [`metric_set_bounded`](../theories/CoveringFamilyUP.v#L29) ([`CoveringFamilyUP.v`](../theories/CoveringFamilyUP.v)), [`covering_zero_radius_distinguishes_balls`](../tests/CoveringExamples.v#L25) ([`CoveringExamples.v`](../tests/CoveringExamples.v)) |
| 2.8.1 | hypothesis or convention | Closedness of the eligible family, measurability of open sets and finiteness on bounded sets are recorded in a separate context predicate; the fine-cover and adequacy predicates can be stated for arbitrary families. | Keeps the standing hypotheses explicit and permits elementary consequences to use only their actual premises. It does not add separability, compactness or Borel regularity. | [`is_covering_theory_context`](../theories/CoveringFamilyUP.v#L37), [`family_covers_finely`](../theories/CoveringFamilyUP.v#L60), [`family_is_measure_adequate`](../theories/CoveringFamilyUP.v#L93) ([`CoveringFamilyUP.v`](../theories/CoveringFamilyUP.v)) |
| 2.8.2 | statement correction (erratum) | The preliminary selected family is empty (erratum p. 3, book p. 142 line 33). | Federer's own correction; the preliminary stage is suppressed, so formal W_0 is printed W_1=V and formal G_0 is printed G_1. | [`is_cover_contraction_exhaustion`](../theories/ContractionCoverUP.v#L56) ([`ContractionCoverUP.v`](../theories/ContractionCoverUP.v)) |

## 2.10 Carathéodory's construction

Items 2.10.1 and 2.10.3–2.10.4 are formalised with the distinctions below. Item 2.10.10 is
proved with the printed hypotheses and argument. The integer-degree
assertions of 2.10.2 are proved; its optional Gamma extension and printed
circle computation remain pending. Earlier supporting ingredients remain
recorded under 2.5.10. Item 2.10.5 is formalised, including normalized-measure monotonicity,
common null sets and open/closed convex equality at every exponent. Items 2.10.7–2.10.9 are also complete with the distinctions below. Item 2.10.6 awaits its forward comparisons and examples. The whole subsection is not complete.
